# Dreamstone Ruination — World Bible

**Status:** Living document, v0.9.7
**Project base:** Fork of `dsmyst/dreamstone-mysteries` (itself a fork of `rh-hideout/pokeemerald-expansion`)
**Working title:** Dreamstone Ruination

> **Legend:** `[LOCKED]` = committed decision · `[WORKING]` = current direction, may refine · `[OPEN]` = unresolved, needs future discussion

> **v0.9.7 patch summary:** Major character expansions (Eden to deuteragonist, Brie Moray to Trial Baroness, Ambrose Caymen as Archbishop of the Arceus Templar, female Rock Vizier, Jordan Ramses + Wakahisa as recurring rivals), the parallel-main-quest Mega-Dreamstone Shard hunt, the Tinkatink dragon-slayer bloodline lore, the Tinkaton Matriarch ancient-sleeper, the Solomonar title, the v0.9.7 Tinkaton stat lock (BST 560), the Behemoth Bash custom move with Steel-effectiveness override, the Vizier gauntlet reorder (Rock → Umbra → Silas → Glaive → Rhydia), and two terminology sweeps: Trial Lord → Trial Baron / Trial Baroness, and Supreme Sage → Lunacy. See full patch notes at end of document.

---

## 1. Canon Contract

### Timeline

| Era | Approximate distance from Gen 1 (RBY) | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Ancient Cormoria (prehistoric) | Thousands of years before our game | Era of the Ancient Terror; Paradox-Pokémon-ancestors exist; protagonist briefly visits via dreamstone resonance (Shiny Slither Wing encounter) |
| AZ's Ultimate Weapon War (Kalos) | ~3,000 years before modern | ~1,000 years before our game. Kalos is in long post-war recovery during our era. |
| **Dreamstone Ruination (our game)** | **~2,000 years before modern** | Old empire era Cormoria. The Paldean Empire is *recently founded and consolidating* — in a dangerous state of flux. |
| The merchant's journey east (epilogue) | Same era as our game | Veddev Amber-Letz (formerly Prince Osrid) sells the four cursed objects to the treasure-loving Paldean king. **This is the canonical event Ms. Raifort teaches in Pokémon Scarlet and Violet.** |
| Treasures of Ruin sealed by ancient Pokémon wielders | Shortly after our game | Paldean ancient hero-wielders seal Wo-Chien, Chien-Pao, Ting-Lu, and Chi-Yu in shrines across Paldea. Osrid and Eden are *implied* to be among the sealers (open-ended). |
| Paldea's long reform | Centuries following our game | The king's catastrophe forces Paldea into slow, painful self-improvement across ~1,500 years. The reform is *incomplete* — underlying inequality persists, which is why the Treasures can awaken again in SV. |
| Despotic dynasties of Kanto/Johto/Hoenn collapse | Across the next ~500–1000 years | Cormorian meritocratic values culturally bleed into KJH; despotic regimes are replaced by early Pokémon League system. |
| Pokémon Legends: Arceus (Hisui era) | ~150–300 years before modern | Volo of the Ginkgo Guild lives here. Descendant of Veddev's adopted line. Kalos and Hisui cultural influences also contribute to the modern Pokémon world. |
| Modern Pokémon games (Gen 1 through Gen 9) | Year 0 reference | Cynthia is a distant descendant of Veddev's adopted line. Professor Tenebris (DM) is a descendant of Umbra. Lance's family descends from Glaive's Dragon's Den order. |
| Pokémon Dreamstone Mysteries | Modern era | ~2,000 years after our game. Team Somber descends from Ruin Cult remnants. The detective academy descends from cult-defectors who turned to opposition. |

### Hard canon constraints (cannot contradict)

- The Treasures of Ruin originated in an unnamed eastern land (Cormoria, in our setting). `[LOCKED]`
- A merchant from the east delivered them to a treasure-loving Paldean king during the early Paldean Empire era. `[LOCKED]`
- The four objects are a sword (Chien-Pao), tablets (Wo-Chien), a vessel (Ting-Lu), and beads (Chi-Yu). `[LOCKED]`
- The objects were born from hatred, grudge, fear, and envy respectively. `[LOCKED]`
- After delivery, the objects awoke, destroyed the king's castle, and were sealed in four Paldean shrines. `[LOCKED]`
- Calyrex is a benevolent grass/psychic king of ancient Galar with healing and telepathic powers. `[LOCKED]`
- Slither Wing is a Paradox Pokémon — the prehistoric ancestor of Volcarona. Encountering one in Ancient Cormoria is canon-compatible. `[LOCKED]`
- The Paldean Empire was founded ~2,000 years before modern. Our game sits in its early, consolidating era. `[LOCKED]`

### Soft canon connections (lore-additive, can stay implicit)

- Vandras Amber-Letz's adopted family produces Volo (PLA) and Cynthia (Gen 4). Family name Amber-Letz → Berlitz via linguistic drift. `[LOCKED]`
- Glaive founds Blackthorn City and the Dragon's Den order. Lance's family descends from that order. Glaive's nickname is *Blackthorn*. `[LOCKED — soft canon]`
- Umbra is the ancestor of Professor Tenebris from Dreamstone Mysteries. Latin-shadow naming tradition. `[LOCKED]`
- Cult remnants eventually become Team Somber (Dreamstone Mysteries villains). `[LOCKED]`
- Cult-defectors who turned to opposition eventually establish the detective academy (Dreamstone Mysteries). `[LOCKED]`
- Eden may have a hand in establishing the proto-detective tradition alongside Veddev/Osrid after Paldean sealing — *implied through her temperament and choices, not stated as canon*. `[LOCKED — implied]`

### Canon humility principle

The Ruin Cults' beliefs about Calyrex and Arceus are *their* beliefs, presented through their texts and rhetoric. Canon does not confirm Calyrex can mind-control all Pokémon. The cults' fanaticism is built on a *plausible misreading* of theology, not literal mechanical truth. `[LOCKED]`

### Thematic thesis `[LOCKED]`

**Pokémon are partners, not weapons. Anything you treat as a weapon eventually becomes a Treasure of Ruin.**

This thesis is *demonstrated* through character arcs (especially Glaive and Osrid), not preached through dialogue. The Treasures of Ruin are the anti-Pokémon — beings born from human emotions who can only be wielded or sealed, never bonded with. Every major character arc refracts this thesis:

- Osrid: soldier-who-commanded → trainer-who-bonds → man-who-chooses-his-own-name-over-his-birth-name
- Glaive: vengeance-driven weapon-trainer → opens his heart → his Shelgon evolves into Salamence
- Eden: thief who *steals* partners → woman who helps *seal* the things that can't be partnered with
- Vandras: merchant of objects → *adopts a person*, recognizing a lost soul is worth more than any object

Note one design tension we hold deliberately: the starter Pokémon are weapons-coded (Baxcalibur, Ursaluna, Tinkaton). The prince begins his trainer journey with creatures that *look* like weapons and must learn they aren't. This is the thesis in microcosm.
