## 8. The Four Ruin Cults

### Origin: The Widening Eye `[LOCKED]`

The four Ruin Cults all descend from a single original order: **The Widening Eye** — the order who first congregated around the Treasures Of Ruin in Cormoria.

The Widening Eye name evokes surveillance, awakening, apocalyptic vision — eyes opening to receive forbidden knowledge. Each successor cult is an *Eye that widened toward a different god*. Each cult sees only its Treasure and is blind to the others.

**The split:** Over time, individual members of the Widening Eye gravitated toward different Treasures. The Treasures themselves — by their alignments — conflicted with one another and competed for devotee influence. This pulled the order apart into four denominations. They retain an uneasy cooperation today (in our era) under the larger goal of transforming Cormoria, but are simultaneously competing for top position in the coming "new world order."

### The nature of the Treasures' influence `[LOCKED]`

The Treasures are *not* fully agentic. They do not have plans, strategies, or conscious wills. But each Treasure exudes a **negative-alignment aura** based on its emotional substrate, and this aura amplifies pre-existing emotional wounds in those susceptible.

**Tolkien analogy:** This is how the Rings of Power work in *The Lord of the Rings*. The Ring does not have plans. But it has a gravitational pull on those susceptible to it. It amplifies what is already there. A wounded heart in the Ring's presence becomes a more wounded heart, then a captured one.

**Implications for the four cults:**
- **Chien-Pao (hatred)** finds people already carrying vengeful hatred — usually those who have lost something to perceived betrayal
- **Wo-Chien (grudge)** finds people who feel they have been wronged, denied, or dismissed by authority — often scholars and recordkeepers
- **Ting-Lu (fear)** finds people living in chronic fear — the poor, the powerless, those who feel hunted — and offers them the inversion of becoming feared themselves
- **Chi-Yu (envy)** finds people who already envy what others have — **Eden's susceptibility makes perfect sense here**

The Treasures do not *create* their followers' emotional substrates. They *find and amplify* pre-existing wounds. This means cult recruitment is highly opportunistic. Paldea's caste system produces an abundance of all four wound-types; this is why Paldea is the recruitment goldmine. Even Cormoria's more meritocratic society produces some.

**Eden's defection mechanism:** As her envy is *resolved* through the journey with Osrid (belonging, recognized skill, shared purpose), Chi-Yu's pull on her *weakens*. She does not have to overcome a magical compulsion through willpower — she becomes susceptible, then unsusceptible, through changes in her actual emotional life. The same mechanism explains why the cults' grip on a defector is breakable but the grip on a Harbinger is permanent: the Harbingers' wounds are too deep.

**Pierra's compromise is a different mechanism.** Her wound is grief, not any of the four Treasures' substrates. She is not a cult devotee. She is *politically compromised by Silas Moray's manipulation*, which is a human-level corruption, not a Treasure-level one. This means **Pierra is redeemable by recovering from grief and recognizing what Silas has done to her** — distinct from the cult arc.

### The Harbingers `[LOCKED]`

**Harbinger** is the title used by each leader of the four cult denominations. The title evokes "one who announces what is coming" — each Harbinger is preparing the world for the awakening of their Treasure.

- **Harbinger Baradus** — leader of the Sword Cult (Chien-Pao). Primary antagonist. Full profile below.
- **Harbinger Alaric** (deceased) — leader of the Vessels Cult (Ting-Lu). Killed by Nemo Korolev ~2 years before the story begins, during the Vessels Cult's Ting-Lu incarnation ritual. Ting-Lu was taken by Nemo in the same operation. The Vessels Cult was dismantled in this operation. `[OPEN]` Alaric's pre-death character details.
- **Harbinger Goma** — leader of the Beads Cult (Chi-Yu). **Female. The only woman who managed to become a Harbinger within the Widening Eye before its four-way split into the four denominational cults.** She is the **best Pokemon trainer in the cults besides Baradus himself** — her combat capability is what kept her alive through the cult-political infighting of the Widening Eye era. Profile detailed in Section 8 (Beads Cult subsection — expanded with Goma's full character).
- **Harbinger of the Tablets Cult** (deceased) — name and backstory `[OPEN]`. **Killed by Baradus** at the Hoya River altar when Baradus ambushed and slaughtered the Tablets Cult after they had successfully incarnated Wo-Chien. Wo-Chien was then seized by Baradus and the Sword Cult.

### The CURRENT state of the cults — corrected landscape `[LOCKED]`

**This is a critical correction from earlier drafts.** By the time of story present, the cult landscape is NOT four active cults gradually being absorbed. It is:

**Two cults destroyed in backstory:**

1. **Tablets Cult: extinct (years before story start).**
   - The Tablets Cult, led by their Harbinger (`[OPEN]` name), succeeded in incarnating Wo-Chien through ritual at an ancient altar in the northern Hoya River area
   - **Baradus ambushed and slaughtered the Tablets Cult at the altar**, using their slaughter as a blood sacrifice that completed the Wo-Chien incarnation and let him seize the Treasure for the Sword Cult
   - **The Sword Cult has held Wo-Chien since before the game begins.** This is a backstory fact that the player learns mid-game.

2. **Vessels Cult: extinct (~2 years before story start).**
   - The Vessels Cult, led by Harbinger Alaric, succeeded in incarnating Ting-Lu through ritual at an ancient altar in a deep forgotten cave system
   - **Nemo Korolev ambushed and killed Alaric** at the moment of incarnation, dismantling the cult in the same operation
   - Nemo took Ting-Lu into his keeping
   - **Baradus and Goma both call the unknown person who did this "the Usurper"** and have spent the past 2 years hunting for them
   - Baradus's gradual infiltration of Cormorian high society is *partly* a manhunt for the Usurper — he believes the Usurper is a powerful Cormorian who is hiding their identity and the Treasure
   - **The Usurper is Nemo Korolev** (Section 7) — the 9th Trial Baron. The player learns this during the 9th Trial battle.

**Two cults active at story start:**

3. **Sword Cult (dominant)** — see expanded profile below
4. **Beads Cult (active but weakened)** — see expanded profile below

This means **at story start there are only two active cult organizations**, with one Treasure (Wo-Chien) already controlled by the Sword Cult since before game start, and one Treasure (Ting-Lu) secretly held by the 9th Trial Baron in symbiotic coexistence outside the cult system entirely.

### The Cult Cosmological Framework — Why the Cults Move Slowly, and What's Truly at Stake `[LOCKED — major v0.9.4 expansion]`

This subsection consolidates the cosmological constraints that dictate cult strategy. Read alongside Section 2 — Cormorian founding myth (Phancero origin), Section 2 — Leyline Resonance, and Section 9 — Cormor's True Status.

#### What the Treasures of Ruin actually are `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

**The Treasures of Ruin are *not* naturally arising cosmic entities.** Per Section 2 — Cormorian founding myth: they are **inverted Dreamstones** that originated as ordinary tribal artifacts.

The four objects (wooden tablets, sword, vessel, beads) were originally:
1. **Tribal artifacts** of the pre-Cormorian nomadic peoples — utilitarian items made of a unique stone material that could store memories and emotional energy (i.e., natural Dreamstones in object form)
2. **Forged by Cormor (the original blacksmith) with Roma's miko-consecration help** for the channeling ritual that would erase Phancero
3. **Charged with Cormor + Keerin's combined cosmic-tier resonance** during the erasing ritual
4. **Inverted over centuries** by their long exposure to Phancero's residual contamination — where natural Dreamstones accumulate *positive* emotional/memory energy, the four objects began absorbing *negative* energy as their power source
5. **Eventually manifested** as Dark-typed Pokemon (Wo-Chien, Chien-Pao, Ting-Lu, Chi-Yu) — *inverted Dreamstones in Pokemon form*

**The Treasures of Ruin are *the same material* as Dreamstones**, just *flipped polarity*. **This is precisely why Dreamstones can resonate with Treasures and serve as incarnation catalysts** — same material; same resonance properties; opposite alignment.

The cults don't know this history. They believe they are pursuing naturally-arising cosmic entities that pre-date Cormoria. **In truth, the Treasures are *Cormoria's own founding-myth residue*.** The cults are unknowing instruments of the very event Cormoria was founded to seal away.

#### The three conditions for incarnating a Treasure of Ruin `[LOCKED]`

1. **Leyline-resonance Location** — the *specific* Leyline-resonance point that matches that Treasure's nature (Section 2 — Leyline Resonance). Each Treasure has a uniquely compatible point. The locations are NOT interchangeable:
   - **Wo-Chien** → an ancient altar in the **Hoya River area** (where Tablets Cult performed their successful incarnation before Baradus slaughtered them)
   - **Ting-Lu** → a deep forgotten **cave system** (where Vessels Cult performed their successful incarnation before Nemo intervened)
   - **Chi-Yu** → the deep lava pool room of the **Ashland Labyrinth at Mt. Ceram** — where the Beads Cult attempted their incarnation in Act III
   - **Chien-Pao** → the hidden **Distortion World gate within the Mirroh Necropolis** — where the Sword Cult attempts their incarnation in the climactic late-game Necropolis Assault. **Baradus does not learn this until late in the story** (see Baradus's discovery beat below).

2. **Dreamstone Catalyst** — a sufficiently powerful Dreamstone to provide the resonance energy. **Now reframed:** Dreamstones are the *same material* as the Treasures of Ruin, just *positive polarity*. **Using a positive Dreamstone to incarnate an inverted Treasure is essentially *re-aligning the Treasure's resonance frequency with positive Dreamstone energy* — temporarily neutralizing the negative-polarity opposition that would otherwise prevent material incarnation.** This is *precisely* the resonance work needed to overcome Arceus's intervention barrier.

3. **Resonance Match** — the timing and ritual must be precisely calibrated to the Treasure's specific signature. Even with the right location and a suitable Dreamstone, an improperly executed ritual fails.

#### The Arceus-avoidance constraint — refined cosmic stakes `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

**This is the crucial cosmological fact:** incarnating a Treasure *outside* its proper Leyline point, or *without* Dreamstone catalysis, would cause universe-shattering side effects that would inevitably draw **Arceus's direct intervention.**

**Per v0.9.4 cosmology, Arceus's non-intervention is *active mercy*** — not absence, not indifference, but a *principled choice* (Section 9 — Arceus and Keerin):
- Arceus believes in **free will** — the choices of created beings matter
- Arceus *will not intervene* in matters that fall within free-will scope, even when those choices produce harm
- Arceus *would intervene* in matters that exceed free-will scope — specifically, threats that pre-date or violate the foundational order of creation itself
- **Phancero is such a threat** — Arceus's own shadow-self, not a free-willed being, not subject to the free-will framework
- **The cults' machinations are *not* such a threat** — they are choices within free-will scope, even if they enable Phancero's eventual return

**Arceus's restraint is therefore *targeted***. It does not act against the cults because the cults are within free-will scope. It *would* act against Phancero — but Phancero is currently erased from the material world and cannot act directly.

**The cosmic stakes of full Treasure incarnation `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`:**

**Phancero was *erased*, not sealed.** Per Section 2 — Cormorian founding myth, the erasing event removed Phancero's incarnated fragment from material reality. **Phancero's true form persists in the void where it has always lived — outside space and time, where the entity originally accumulated as Arceus's shadow.**

**Full incarnation of all four Treasures of Ruin would create a *new pathway* for Phancero to re-emerge:**

1. **Resonance amplification** — four fully-incarnated inverted-Dreamstone vessels resonating in unison would create a cosmic-tier signal that **Phancero in its true form would detect across the void**

2. **Pathway opening** — the Treasures' combined resonance could provide Phancero with a *homing channel* to push more of itself back into material reality

3. **Distortion gate hijack risk** — Phancero could force its way through one of the existing Distortion World gates, using the Treasures' resonance as a beacon and amplifier. **The Mirroh Necropolis Distortion gate is uniquely vulnerable** because it is *adjacent to* a Treasure of Ruin incarnation site (Chien-Pao's Leyline point). If the Sword Cult successfully incarnates Chien-Pao at the gate, **the gate becomes Phancero's most plausible entry pathway.**

**If Phancero begins to re-emerge, Arceus would be forced to intervene directly.** At that scale, the response would be *cosmically catastrophic* — likely **wiping out creation entirely** as collateral damage of the intervention. The cosmic stakes of the cult war are therefore:

- *Either Phancero comes back and warps reality into glitch-chaos*
- *Or Arceus intervenes and the cleanup may obliterate the universe*

**Both outcomes destroy everything.** The player's task in Act VI is to prevent the conditions that would force either outcome.

**Arceus's non-intervention is therefore *mercy with limits*.** Arceus is choosing to give the world time to resolve this through mortal effort. **If the cults push too far, that mercy ends.** This is why **Baradus and Goma are unknowing instruments of cosmic destruction** — they think they pursue power; they are actually pursuing the conditions that would unmake the world.

**The player is *literally saving creation* in the 7v9 final battle.** The stakes have never been "stop a power-hungry cult." The stakes have *always* been "prevent the cosmological catastrophe of either Phancero re-emergence or Arceus's universe-ending intervention." **This is the deepest layer of cosmological-narrative stakes the player can have in any Pokemon project.**

#### The cult timeline — dictated by these constraints `[LOCKED]`

This constraint structure dictates the cult timeline:
- The cults cannot move fast and crude — they must move slow and precise
- They must spend years surveying Leyline locations, identifying which point matches which Treasure
- They must acquire intact, sufficiently powerful Dreamstones (not fragments)
- They must calibrate ritual timing precisely

**The 10+ year cult timeline is dictated by Arceus-avoidance.** If Baradus and Goma were willing to risk Arceus's intervention, they could pursue faster incarnation methods. But Arceus's wrath would unmake whatever they built, so they accept the slow long-game. **Their efficiency is also their cage.**

#### Baradus's late-game discovery of the Necropolis gate `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

For most of the cult timeline, **the Sword Cult does NOT know where Chien-Pao's Leyline point is.** They have been searching for it across years of effort. The cult operates from a hidden HQ (location never specified in the bible; the player never visits it).

**Late in the story (likely Act V or early Act VI), Baradus discovers** that the Distortion gate to Giratina is guarded in the deepest parts of the Mirroh Necropolis grave. **He makes the contextual connection: this Distortion gate must be Chien-Pao's Leyline point.**

`[OPEN]` How exactly Baradus discovers the Necropolis gate's existence. The most poignant design is **through Silas Moray's Vizier-of-Commerce intelligence network indirectly leaking the knowledge** — Silas's commerce-spy infrastructure produces information Baradus correlates, making Silas the *unknowing enabler* of the climactic cult action. This deepens Silas's tragic-redemption arc — he sets the stage for the cosmic-tier crisis without knowing it.

**Once Baradus identifies the Necropolis gate**, he pivots the cult's entire focus toward seizing it. **This becomes the climactic operation** — the unified Necropolis Assault that opens Act VI and culminates in the 7v9 final battle (Section 10 — Act VI; Section 7 — Kimaris Langerin).

The cult's prior years of intelligence-gathering, infiltration, and political-economic positioning all serve the *single goal* of getting the cult to the Necropolis at the right moment with sufficient force to penetrate House Langerin's defense. **The Necropolis Assault is everything the Sword Cult has been building toward.**

#### The Dreamstone-fragment failure that derailed Tablets and Vessels Cults `[LOCKED]`

A critical historical fact: **the Tablets Cult and the Vessels Cult both succeeded at incarnating their Treasures — but they used Dreamstone *fragments*, and those fragments turned to dust during the incarnation process.** The fragments were one-use catalysts; they were consumed in the ritual.

Baradus and Goma couldn't reuse what was left:
- **Baradus seized Wo-Chien opportunistically** by slaughtering the Tablets Cult, but **got no usable Dreamstone catalyst from the operation.** He has been searching for a sufficiently powerful intact Dreamstone ever since.
- **Goma got *nothing* from the Vessels Cult's success.** Nemo killed Alaric and took both Ting-Lu and whatever remained of the Dreamstone fragments (which had already turned to dust). Goma has been searching for *her* Dreamstone independently.

**This is why Goma is so desperate.** She has been the only one of the four original Harbingers who has *never* succeeded at incarnation. The Tablets Cult succeeded (then was destroyed). The Vessels Cult succeeded (then was destroyed). Baradus *opportunistically* gained Wo-Chien through slaughter. **Goma has accomplished the least.**

Her resentment of Baradus is partly that *he at least has Wo-Chien* while she has nothing. Her envy of the dead Tablets and Vessels Harbingers is *real* — they accomplished what she has been unable to accomplish, even though they died for it. **She would trade her life for one successful incarnation, if she could be sure her Beads Cult would continue under her successor.**

#### Nemo's true historical significance — confirmed by this framework `[LOCKED]`

Per Section 7 — Nemo Korolev: **Nemo's interception of Ting-Lu is the single act that delayed the cult takeover of Cormoria by 2+ critical years.** Without Nemo:
- Baradus would have acquired Ting-Lu through his standard ambush methodology
- The Sword Cult would have held *two* Treasures
- With two Treasures + 10 years of cult infiltration + Sylphon & Stone funding + Silas's manipulation of Pierra — **the cults would have taken over Cormoria long before Osrid was forced to return.**

**Nemo is the secret savior who does not realize what he saved.** He is the most consequential individual in Cormoria's recent history in terms of what he prevented.

### Sword Cult (Chien-Pao) `[LOCKED]`

- **The dominant active cult.** Has been since absorbing the Tablets Cult and taking Wo-Chien before the game began.
- Led by **Harbinger Baradus** (full profile below)
- **Second-in-command: Silas Moray, Vizier of Commerce** (full profile in Section 7)
- Already controls **Wo-Chien** (seized from the slaughtered Tablets Cult). Their ultimate goal is incarnating **Chien-Pao**, the cult's namesake Treasure that has not yet been incarnated.
- **Baradus's ultimate plan: trigger Chien-Pao's incarnation through a specific resonance configuration** that requires the other three Treasures plus a Dreamstone, under conditions only Baradus fully understands. **This is one of the central mysteries of the story.** As the cult hunts for Ting-Lu (with Nemo), partially revives and re-pursues Chi-Yu, and prepares the Wo-Chien they already hold, the player gradually pieces together what the final incarnation requires.
- Across the game: hunts "the Usurper" (Nemo Korolev), penetrates Cormorian high society through Silas, manipulates Empress Pierra through Silas's psychic powers, slowly destabilizes the empire from within while preparing the Chien-Pao incarnation
- Aesthetic: bladed weapons, blood-and-snow imagery, vengeance theology, anti-Arceus/anti-Calyrex creed
- `[OPEN]` Geographic stronghold (the secret HQ where they hold Nemo captive late-game)
- `[OPEN]` Rank-and-file grunt design

### Beads Cult (Chi-Yu) — Active, Weakened, Goma-Led `[LOCKED]`

**Organizational structure:**
- Led by **Harbinger Goma**
- **Bishop** — mid-tier rank, leading a small group of acolytes (chess-piece organizational naming convention)
- **Acolyte** — entry-level grunt rank
- `[OPEN]` Other ranks between Bishop and Harbinger; specific Beads Cult terminology for advanced operatives

**Eden's former cult.** She was recruited by them, then defected (Section 7 — Eden).

**Cult ideology and aesthetic:** Ornamentation, wealth-display, fire-and-jewel imagery, envy theology. The Chi-Yu Treasure embodies *envy stoked by multiple conflicts*; the cult's spiritual framework celebrates envy and material accumulation as paths to the Chi-Yu incarnation.

Has NOT yet successfully incarnated Chi-Yu at story start.

### Harbinger Goma — Beads Cult leader, the cunning rival `[LOCKED]`

**Background:**
- **Female. The only woman who managed to become a Harbinger within the Widening Eye before the cult's four-way denominational split.** This is a singular distinction — the Widening Eye era was even more aggressively male-dominated than the post-split cult organizations, and Goma reached the top through sheer competence and survival.
- `[OPEN]` Goma's pre-cult background (ethnic origin, family history, what drew her to the Widening Eye)
- **The best Pokemon trainer in the cults besides Baradus himself.** Her combat capability is what has kept her alive across cult-political infighting and external opposition. She has outlasted countless rivals — male and female alike — through her trainer skill.

**Hatred for Baradus:**
- **Goma has always hated Baradus** for his demeaning sense of superiority over all others and his unwillingness to bend or compromise on anything he didn't come up with
- The hatred predates the Widening Eye's split — they have been at each other's throats for years within the same organization, then within the same broader cult ecosystem after the split
- **The Beads Cult's failure to coordinate with the Sword Cult is largely Goma's choice.** She refuses to align with Baradus's projects on principle. If Baradus suggests something, Goma's instinct is to do the opposite or pursue an independent path.
- This is one of Cormoria's *quiet advantages*: **the cults' disunity has been one of Rhydia's protective barriers** across the past decade. They have been undercutting each other for years; Goma and Baradus's mutual loathing has prevented the kind of unified cult crisis that would have toppled the empire long ago.

**Strategic implication:** Despite both cults pursuing Treasure-of-Ruin objectives, **Goma's Beads Cult and Baradus's Sword Cult operate as effective rivals.** Baradus has been pursuing the slow long-game (Wo-Chien held since before the story, Silas's infiltration of Cormorian high society, the Chien-Pao incarnation conditions). Goma has been pursuing parallel, *independent* paths to Chi-Yu's incarnation — without Baradus's resources, without Sword Cult coordination, often *against* Sword Cult interests when their paths cross.

**The envy at the core of Goma `[LOCKED]`:**

Goma's psychological center is *envy* — fitting, since the Beads Cult's theology celebrates envy as a path to Chi-Yu's incarnation (Section 8 — Beads Cult), and Chi-Yu itself canonically embodies envy stoked by multiple conflicts. **The Harbinger of the cult of envy is herself consumed by it.**

Goma envies:
- **Baradus** — who has Wo-Chien (acquired through opportunistic slaughter of the Tablets Cult) while she has nothing. Her hatred of his personality compounds the underlying envy of his strategic accomplishment.
- **The dead Tablets Harbinger and Alaric** — both *succeeded at incarnating their Treasures* before they were destroyed. *They accomplished what she has been unable to accomplish.* They died for it, but the accomplishment is real. **Goma is the only one of the four original Harbingers who has *never* succeeded at incarnation.**
- **Justice's lineage** — the royal family possesses a Mythical-tier asset by birthright. The Beads Cult's entire purpose is to create cosmic-tier power through *envy*; Justice exists as a constant reminder that the royal family already has what the cults are dying to obtain.

**Her envy fuels her competence.** Goma is one of the few characters in the project whose *signature flaw* is also the *engine* of her capability. Her envy drives her to outperform — to be a better trainer than the male Harbingers who looked down on her, to be more cunning than Baradus, to succeed where the dead Harbingers failed. **The same psychological pattern that makes her dangerous makes her ultimately doomed** — envy is, by its nature, a hunger that cannot be satisfied even by success. Even if Goma had successfully incarnated Chi-Yu, her envy would have found a new object.

**She would trade her life for one successful incarnation, if she could be sure her Beads Cult would continue under her successor.** This is the psychological core that makes her late-game return (Section 8 — Goma's late-game return mechanism `[OPEN]`) dangerous: a Harbinger with nothing left to lose, willing to spend her life on a final desperate attempt to accomplish *something*.

**Goma's two major operations in the game:**

**Operation 1: The Royal Palace Assassination Plot `[LOCKED]` (post-Trial 2)**

The first of Goma's major moves in the present-day story. Two objectives:
- **Assassinate Rhydia** — Rhydia's growing popularity has become a political threat to the cults' creeping influence in Cormoria. Removing her would destabilize the empire dramatically.
- **Seize the Dreamstone hidden in the royal palace** — needed for the Beads Cult's planned Chi-Yu incarnation ritual through resonance

The player participates in the response to this plot (Section 7 — Rhydia; Section 10 — Story Spine) — including the **doubles-format scripted battles** alongside Rhydia against Beads Cult acolytes and one Bishop.

**The plot fails.** Rhydia survives. The Dreamstone is preserved in the palace. Goma escapes.

**Operation 2: The Mt. Ceram Lava-Pool Operation `[LOCKED]` (~70% through)**

**Caused by Operation 1's failure.** When the palace plan fails, Goma escalates to a more desperate strategy: invade Mt. Ceram, use the lava-pool room deep in the Ashland Labyrinth as the resonance chamber, and revive Chi-Yu through that environment instead of through the palace Dreamstone.

This is the operation that culminates in:
- The Ashland Labyrinth raid
- The Dreamstone resonance going out of control
- The displacement of player+Reid+Chi-Yu into Ancient Cormoria
- Koraidon's destruction of Chi-Yu's body with Breaking Swipe
- The seizure of the singed Beads of Ruin by the player party
- **Goma's escape** — she becomes a desperate antagonist for the remainder of the story

**Why this escalation is structurally important:**
- **Goma is not just doing random cult stuff.** She has a coherent objective (incarnate Chi-Yu) that she pursues through escalating methods. *She is failing forward.*
- **She is acting alone**, without Baradus's resources or coordination — which justifies why the Mt. Ceram operation is so reckless and unsupported
- **The failed assassination stains her in the Cormorian public consciousness.** By Mt. Ceram, she is the most hated woman in the empire even though most Cormorians have never seen her face. Her late-game return will land with weight.

**Combat strength:**
- Goma is the strongest trainer the player will face among the cult organizations besides Baradus himself
- **Goma's personal type specialty: Fighting `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`** — *deliberately* chosen to counter Baradus's Ice signature. Fighting is the canonical Pokemon type-counter to Ice (Fighting is 2× into Ice; Fighting moves bypass Ice's defensive coverage in ways most other types cannot). **Goma trained herself into a Fighting-type master *specifically to be able to beat Baradus in a hypothetical confrontation* — her envy-of-Baradus and her hatred-of-Baradus together produced a personal-mastery decision that is *another expression of her envy-as-engine* psychological pattern.** She did not just want to be a better Harbinger; she wanted to be the *type-specific threat* to the man she most resents.
- **Her cult subordinates lean Fire and Dark** (the cult's broader thematic register — Chi-Yu is Dark/Fire), but **Goma personally specializes in Fighting**. This *mismatch* between leader and rank-and-file is itself characterizing — Goma's Beads Cult is Fire/Dark in its institutional flavor but Fighting in its leadership ace, a duality that other Harbingers do not have.
- **Primary ace: Smolder** — shiny **Emboar** (Fire/Fighting). The dual-type Smolder doubles as both a *cult-thematic Fire* Pokemon (matching the Beads Cult's Chi-Yu flavor) and Goma's *personal-mastery Fighting* ace. *One Pokemon, two thematic functions.* Smolder is genuinely top-tier — Emboar's BST of 528 with its Reckless or Blaze ability and its diverse coverage (Flare Blitz, Close Combat, Wild Charge, Earthquake) makes for a brutal physical sweeper.
- **Secondary ace: Mega Houndoom** (Dark/Fire). Cult-thematic, preserves the Dark/Fire visual register of the Beads Cult institutional flavor, gives Goma a powerful special-attack option (Solar Power Mega Houndoom in sun setup is canonically devastating). **Goma's possession of Mega Evolution capability is itself politically loaded** — Mega Evolution is state-sanctioned in Cormoria (Section 2 — Mega Evolution policy), and *Goma having a Mega Stone* means she either acquired it pre-disgrace (when the Beads Cult had Sylphon & Stone funding) or stole it from a state-sanctioned holder. Either way, *her Mega Houndoom is the visible evidence that the cult has historically operated with resources the empire never authorized.*
- Rest of her Trial-tier roster `[OPEN]` — likely additional Fighting-types reflecting her personal mastery, possibly cult-thematic Fire or Dark types reflecting her institutional role
- `[OPEN]` Whether she has a generational/heirloom Pokemon comparable to the senior noble houses' generational aces (likely no — she is not Cormorian noble; her team is *constructed elite* rather than *inherited elite*)

**Late-game return `[OPEN]`:**
- After Mt. Ceram, Goma escapes and becomes desperate
- Her late-game return mechanism is still `[OPEN]` — possibilities include allying with Baradus despite mutual hatred (existential threat overrides their feud), attempting one more solo Chi-Yu revival path (after which she dies), or being the final cult agent the player must confront before the Sword Cult HQ raid
- Whatever form her return takes, it should reflect *desperation* — Goma at her most dangerous because she has nothing left to lose

### Tablets Cult (Wo-Chien) — DESTROYED IN BACKSTORY `[LOCKED]`

- **Existed before the game; extinct by story start**
- Originally built around the cursed tablets, born from a punished documenter's grudge
- Aesthetic (historical): scholarly, archival, document-and-text imagery
- **Successfully incarnated Wo-Chien** through ritual at the Hoya River altar
- **Slaughtered by Baradus and the Sword Cult** at the same altar, in the same operation — their death-blood completed the incarnation conditions
- **Wo-Chien was seized by Baradus** at the slaughter
- The player learns this backstory during mid-game when Wo-Chien is encountered/witnessed in Sword Cult control

### Vessels Cult (Ting-Lu) — DESTROYED IN BACKSTORY `[LOCKED]`

- **Existed until ~2 years before story start; extinct by story start**
- Originally built around the cursed vessel, born from human fear accumulated over generations
- Aesthetic (historical): ritual, ceremonial vessels, earth-and-stone imagery
- Led by **Harbinger Alaric** (`[OPEN]` further pre-death details)
- **Successfully incarnated Ting-Lu** through ritual at an ancient altar in a deep forgotten cave system
- **Ambushed and dismantled by Nemo Korolev** at the same altar in the same operation — Nemo killed Alaric, took Ting-Lu
- Has been in homeostatic symbiosis with Nemo for 2 years (Section 7 — Nemo)
- The player learns this backstory when Nemo's identity as the Usurper is revealed during the 9th Trial

### Harbinger Baradus — leader of the Sword Cult `[LOCKED]`

**The primary antagonist of the project. Theological, irredeemable, deeply personal.**

**Origin: the Yo'aki tribe of Tellius.**
- Born into the **Yo'aki tribe** in the region of **Tellius** (cross-fandom reference to the *Pokemon Empire* fan project — see Section 12)
- A warrior culture, distinct from Cormoria's in important ways (specifics `[OPEN]`)
- Was an exceptional warrior — skilled, cunning, with the operational discipline of his Yo'aki tradition
- **Betrayed his family** for reasons that remain undisclosed in the game. (The unspoken nature of the betrayal is itself character; whatever it was, even Baradus doesn't fully articulate it in story.)
- **Exiled rather than executed** — and this is the *active humiliation*, not the betrayal itself. Execution would have given him a warrior's end. Exile said: *you are not worth even killing.* That distinction is the wound the Sword found.

**Pre-existing worldview (predates Treasure influence):**
- **Zero-sum view of the world** — for him to rise, others must fall; for one to be free, others must be enslaved; competition is the only honest relationship between beings
- This was already in him before any Treasure touched him — the Treasure didn't create his nihilism, it *amplified* his nihilism into cosmology

**The journey: Tellius → Kanto → Cormoria.**
- After exile, Baradus did not come directly to Cormoria
- He **spent time in Kanto first**, where he built his Ice-focused team and acquired Articuno
- He held a **public-facing identity** in Kanto for several years — likely an academic, mercenary, or low-profile professional persona. He has a Kantoan paper trail under that persona that could theoretically be investigated by Cormorian intelligence (`[OPEN]` whether this becomes a story thread)
- Then quietly left Kanto, on his own terms, without burning bridges
- Made his way to **Cormoria** with his prepared Ice team and his secret Ultra Beast companion
- His Kanto period is **referenced briefly in-game but never deeply explored** — flavor depth, not a major worldbuilding thread

**Guzzlord — his oldest companion (pre-exile):**
- Baradus has had Guzzlord since his **Yo'aki past**, before exile, before any of his current theology
- They have been together for ~20+ years
- **The one relationship in his life that wasn't a weapon** — the only continuity across his transformation from Yo'aki warrior → exile → cult leader → Sword's vessel
- Guzzlord is the *only* living witness to Baradus's pre-exile self
- **Guzzlord is difficult to control for extended periods in the real world** (as an Ultra Beast). Baradus keeps it boxed most of the time, deployed only at strategic moments. **Beartic is the public-facing ace; Guzzlord is the strategic-deployment ace.**
- This light touch on the Tellius/Yo'aki connection also implies that the Yo'aki tribe (or Tellius generally) has *some* relationship with Ultra Space — but the project does not commit to specifics. Background flavor only.

**Treasure resonance:**
- The Sword of Ruin (Chien-Pao, not yet incarnate) *felt* his blend of skill + zero-sum philosophy + humiliation-wound and chose him as compatible
- The amplification went both ways: Baradus's grievances became *cosmic*, and the Sword's malignant desire to dominate gained Baradus's tactical brilliance and Yo'aki warrior tradecraft

**Theology — the chosen-one delusion:**
- He has convinced himself he is a **chosen one** destined to "free both humans and Pokemon"
- His named oppressors: **Calyrex** (representing the soul-binding pact between humans and Pokemon — Calyrex is the original consenting partnership and thus the cosmic example of mutual obligation) **and Arceus** (representing creation itself, the source of the order Baradus rejects)
- His thesis: **only Chien-Pao is fit to rule** — a cursed sword as the world's true sovereign
- This is *perfectly* heretical to the Templar Arceus order's worldview (Section 4 — Templar Arceus Order), which makes their opposition to him doctrinally as well as politically necessary
- His battle is not just for Cormoria — it is a battle for the validity of the universe's existing partnership-order against an alternative *anti-partnership* cosmology

**Why he is irredeemable (unlike Silas):**
- His resentment, shame, and inferiority are *now indistinguishable* from the Sword's nihilism. He cannot tell where his self ends and the Treasure begins.
- The exile-wound, the chosen-one delusion, and the cosmic-liberator framing have *compounded* into a singular conviction that admits no doubt
- Unlike Silas (whose delusion was love misshaped into control — *fixable* once love finds its true object), Baradus's delusion is *self-reinforcing*. Every act of evil he commits is *proof* of his chosen status. He cannot lose without losing his entire ontology.
- Therefore he must be *defeated*, not redeemed. There is no Silas-style awakening available for him.

**The three fears — Baradus's fatal flaw `[LOCKED]`:**

For all his cunning and capability, **Baradus only legitimately fears three things in the entire story:**

1. **Justice** — Rhydia's shiny Iron Valiant (Section 7 — Rhydia, Section 9 — Justice combat profile). The single most dangerous Pokemon-tier opponent he can imagine. The royal heirloom's existence is what dictated the cults' long-game strategy — Baradus assesses Justice as capable of eliminating his entire organization in one fell swoop if he makes a major miscalculation.

2. **Calyrex** — the canonical mythical king-Pokemon, representing the soul-binding partnership pact between humans and Pokemon at cosmic scale. **Calyrex is the embodiment of the project's central thesis** (Pokemon-as-partners-not-weapons), made cosmic. Baradus's theology is *explicitly* anti-Calyrex — he sees Calyrex as the original consenting partnership and thus the cosmic example of mutual obligation he is rebelling against (Section 8 — Baradus theology, above). **He understands the thesis he opposes and fears it specifically.** This is sophisticated villainy — he is not a generic anti-hero; he is an *anti-thesis fanatic* who recognizes the cosmic principle he is rebelling against.

3. **Arceus** — creation itself. Baradus's entire methodology is shaped by Arceus-avoidance (Section 8 — Cult Cosmological Framework). The slow 10+ year cult timeline exists *because* of his Arceus-fear. Direct intervention by Arceus would unmake whatever he has built. **His efficiency is also his cage** — he cannot move faster than Arceus-avoidance allows.

**Baradus dismisses everything else.** He dismisses:
- **Osrid completely throughout the story** — he sees Osrid as just another Cormorian noble, never recognizes him as a serious threat even at the climax
- **Most other major trainers** — Nemo (until Nemo intervenes; even then, he hunts Nemo as "the Usurper" without understanding what he has lost), Glaive, Kimaris (despite knowing Marshadow exists), the Trial Barons, the Viziers, the Champion-defeated Champion-tier players
- **Crabominable** — which is *why* the Crabominable adoption scene works narratively. He *discards* Crabominable because Crabominable is, in his estimation, *not even worth fearing*. He doesn't realize that an adopted Crabominable in the player's hands could land the final blow against him — *because Crabominable is precisely the kind of Pokemon he has trained himself to ignore.*

**The structural significance of Baradus's tunnel vision:**

**His three-fears framework is the strategic optimization that becomes his strategic blindness.** He has trained himself to focus only on cosmic-tier threats. This is *efficient* against the three things he fears. It is *catastrophic* against everything below them.

- The player who beats him with a *non-cosmic-tier team* is beating him *through the gap in his attention*. **He never sees the killing blow coming because he was looking up at the gods.**
- His tunnel vision is what makes the project's central thesis (the player's compassion for Crabominable) *deadly* against him. He has dismissed the very Pokemon he needs to fear.
- His tunnel vision is what makes Silas's betrayal possible. Silas is "just" a manipulator-Vizier in Baradus's estimation, not a cosmic threat — so Baradus does not anticipate the moment when Silas's psychic power constrains him. Silas's intervention in the sealing cataclysm (Section 10) is precisely the kind of below-the-cosmic-threshold action Baradus is unable to anticipate.
- His tunnel vision is what makes the entire human-scale resistance to him possible. He has dismissed Nemo, the Trial Barons, the Viziers (except Silas, his asset), the Concordance, the merchant guilds, the Templars — *everyone* who is actually doing the work of holding Cormoria together. He sees only the three cosmic deterrents. Everything else escapes his attention until it's too late.

**This is sophisticated tragic villainy.** Baradus's optimization is *real* — he is genuinely efficient at managing cosmic-tier threats. His blindness is the *unavoidable cost* of that optimization. To see the things below his attention threshold, he would have to abandon the focus that makes him dangerous at all. **He cannot see the partnership thesis without abandoning his anti-partnership theology.** And he will not abandon it. So he dies blind to what kills him.

**Baradus's Stage 1 (mid-game encounter) team — Snow/Hail strategy with Crabominable:**

Earlier in the game (mid-game, ~65-75% through, between Mt. Ceram and Nemo's Trial), the player has a **second encounter with Baradus** (the first encounter being early-game introductions). Player wins this fight. Baradus's team at this encounter includes a **shiny Crabominable**.

**The discard scene — one of the project's signature thesis moments:**
- After the loss, Baradus enters a fanatical rage that he could lose to a "nameless worthless nobody" like the player
- **He blames his loss on Crabominable** and discards it in front of the player — cruelly, theatrically, with mocking dialogue
- This is *the cult worldview made visible*: Baradus treats his Pokémon as instruments that have failed him, not as partners who fought beside him
- **The player has the option to approach Crabominable, offer it food and drink, and build a friendship.**
- If the player chooses to befriend, Crabominable joins the party — **shiny, holding a Punching Glove, with maxed IVs and 252/129/129 EV optimization in Attack/Defense/Special Defense** (Crabominable's response to the player's compassion brings out its full potential — potential that Baradus failed to see)
- The player can choose: nature, ability (Slush Rush / Iron Fist / Defiant), and nickname
- The three ability choices each create a distinct strategic identity:
  - **Slush Rush** — the irony build (Baradus's own weather powers his discarded Pokemon)
  - **Iron Fist** — the punch-power build (1.2× damage on punching moves, no weather needed)
  - **Defiant** (hidden ability) — the vengeance build (+2 Attack when any stat is lowered, mechanically embodying "I refuse to be diminished")

**Why this scene is thematically critical:**
- It is the project's central thesis ("Pokémon are partners, not weapons") played as a single playable moment
- Baradus's discard demonstrates the antithesis literally
- The player's compassion embodies the thesis literally
- The IV/EV mechanic encodes the thesis at the *cellular* level: bond-with-the-trainer physically improves the Pokémon's biology
- It is also the cleanest example of **Principle 2 (Permanent Consequential Choice)** — players who walk past Crabominable have made a different choice than players who take it in. Both choices have downstream consequences.

**Baradus's Stage 1 (final battle, 6v6) team — LOCKED:**

At the final battle (Section 10 — Story Spine), Baradus's main 6-Pokémon team is Ice-focused with Snow/Hail synergy:
1. **Shardik** — *shiny Beartic* (the false ace, public-facing). Ability: **Tough Claws** (bred over generations like other major-character aces — non-canonical for Beartic). Primary sweeper, terrifying under weather support.
2. **Abomasnow** — Ability: **Snow Warning** (auto-Snow on switch-in). Primary weather setter.
3. **Aurorus** — Ability: **Refrigerate** (Normal moves become Ice-type, +20% damage). Likely runs **Hyper Voice** (90 BP Normal → Ice + Refrigerate + STAB = ~162 BP effective Ice spread move hitting both opponents in doubles).
4. **Articuno** — Ability: **Snow Cloak** (+25% evasion in Snow). Acquired in Kanto during Baradus's Kanto period. Tank-defender, possibly running Snowscape (backup weather), Roost, Freeze-Dry, Ice Beam.
5. **Froslass** — Ability: **Snow Cloak**. Fast disruptor — likely Will-O-Wisp + Hex combo, possibly Destiny Bond. Ice/Ghost typing gives Fighting/Normal immunities.
6. **Alolan Sandslash** — Ability: **Slush Rush** (Speed doubles in Snow). Ice/Steel typing is *defensively exceptional* (one weakness: Fire). Fast sweeper at 130 effective Speed under Snow.

**Strategic implications:**
- Two weather setters (Abomasnow primary, Articuno backup) ensure Snow persistence
- Two Slush Rush sweepers (Sandslash, plus Crabominable if Baradus had kept it) outspeed most opponents
- **No member of this team is 4× weak to Fighting except Sandslash** — and Froslass is Ghost-immune to Fighting. Bringing Fighting-type counters is *partial*, not silver-bullet.
- **Fire counters work cleanly on Abomasnow, Aurorus, Sandslash, Beartic** — Reid Ashland's Fire House (Section 7) is mechanically a strategic asset for players who befriend the Ashlands
- The team's frustration factor is high (Snow Cloak evasion, Hyper Voice spread, Will-O-Wisp burn) — **Baradus is designed to be miserable to fight.** That fits his theology: he wins through grinding the player into submission, not clean offensive thunderstrikes.

**Baradus's Stage 2 (final battle, true second phase) team — LOCKED:**

Immediately after Stage 1 (no breaks, no healing), Baradus reveals his true secret weapons:

1. **Wo-Chien** (the Treasure of Tablets — held since before game start) — regenerated and re-incarnate. Dark/Grass typing.
2. **Chi-Yu** (the Treasure of Beads — destroyed by Koraidon at Mt. Ceram, regenerated by the time of the final battle) — regenerated and re-incarnate. Dark/Fire typing.
   - **The Treasures can regenerate under the right conditions.** Koraidon's Breaking Swipe at Mt. Ceram was a delay, not a permanent defeat. **This justifies why *sealing* — not destruction — is the only solution.** Destruction doesn't work. Only sealing works.
3. **Chien-Pao** (the Sword Cult's namesake Treasure, finally incarnated) — the cult's ultimate ambition realized. Dark/Ice typing.
4. **Guzzlord** — Baradus's true ace, brought out of the box. **Shiny, with bred ability Dragon's Maw.**

**Guzzlord's signature move: Ravage** (NEW custom move, see Section 9 — Key Artifacts and Mechanics)
- **130 BP / 85% accuracy / Dragon-type / Physical / no drawbacks**
- Customized version of Dragon Hammer animation for visual gravitas
- With Dragon's Maw (1.5×) + STAB (1.5×): effective BP ~292
- Designed as **Guzzlord's signature delete button**

**Player-side counter-strategy by starter:**
- **Frigibax pickers:** Ice-type Baxcalibur is *thematically born* to fight Dragon-type Ultra Beasts. Mega Baxcalibur's Ice STAB punishes Guzzlord's Dragon-type weakness. (Frigibax-pickers struggle in Stage 1 against Baradus's Ice team — Ice doesn't beat Ice.)
- **Tinkatink pickers:** Tinkaton is Fairy/Steel. **Fairy is immune to Dragon (Guzzlord's Ravage does 0× damage).** Tinkaton resists most of Baradus's Stage 1 team too. Tinkatink pickers have the **best survivability matchup** in both stages.
- **Teddiursa pickers:** Bloodmoon Ursaluna's massive HP pool + Ground/Normal coverage handles both stages through bulk + Earth Power chip damage.

**Each starter has a meaningful role in the final battle.** No starter is left without a path to victory.

**Crabominable in the final battle (if adopted):**
- Adopted Crabominable can synergize with Baradus's own Snow setup (Slush Rush) or fight on its own merits (Iron Fist, Defiant)
- **Fighting/Ice typing is the most lethal possible matchup** against most of Baradus's Stage 1 team
- **Can potentially defeat Chien-Pao** (Fighting 2× super-effective against Dark, with STAB + Punching Glove + ability multipliers)
- Baradus's pre-battle dialogue **shifts if Crabominable is in the player's party** — he mocks the adoption as "refrigerated leftovers" before threatening to kill Osrid and the rest of his family
- **Specific conditional dialogue beats fire** if Crabominable specifically defeats Chien-Pao or Beartic (Shardik) — closing the irony loop
- If Crabominable lands the final blow on Baradus, there is a small additional dialogue acknowledgement

**The dramatic peak of Baradus's irredeemability:**
- He has the form of a relationship (Guzzlord) but cannot model partnership
- He discards Crabominable in rage, demonstrating the antithesis of the project's thesis
- His final dialogue threatens not just to kill the player but to kill the player's mother and sister — *explicit threat escalation* that sets up Silas's death-protecting-Pierra arc
- He cannot see that the discarded Crabominable has returned to fight him; he can only see *his original judgment of Crabominable as worthless*. **The player's compassion offends him.**
- He is irredeemable because he cannot conceive of value-judgments different from his own

## 9. Key Artifacts and Mechanics

### The Dreamstone `[LOCKED — substantially expanded v0.9.4]`

A source of resonance power — but per v0.9.4 cosmology, Dreamstones are now precisely understood:

**What Dreamstones are `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`:**

- **A unique stone material** that can *store memories and emotional energy*. The same material the four original tribal artifacts that became the Treasures of Ruin were made from (Section 2 — Cormorian founding myth).
- **Natural Dreamstones accumulate *positive* emotional and memory energy** across millennia
- **Inverted Dreamstones (the Treasures of Ruin) accumulate *negative* emotional and memory energy** — same material, *opposite polarity*

**The polarity framework `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`:**

| Polarity | Form | Energy Source | Examples |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Positive** | Natural Dreamstones | Accumulated positive memories/emotions across millennia | The Dreamstone the player encounters; the Royal Palace Dreamstone; Slither Wing encounter site; Mt. Silver encounter (with Keerin) |
| **Negative** | Inverted Dreamstones | Accumulated negative memories/emotions / Phancero's residual contamination | The four Treasures of Ruin (Wo-Chien, Chien-Pao, Ting-Lu, Chi-Yu) — *same material*, *flipped polarity* |

**This explains why Dreamstones resonate with Treasures and serve as incarnation catalysts** — they are the same material at different polarities. The cults use positive Dreamstones to *re-align* the negative-polarity Treasures' resonance frequency, enabling material incarnation without drawing Arceus's intervention (Section 8 — Cult Cosmological Framework).

**Dreamstone capabilities `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`:**

- **Resonance catalysis** — can resonate with the four inverted Treasures, enabling their material incarnation
- **Time-displacement** — sufficiently powerful Dreamstones can open paths through time. **Confirmed in-project examples:**
  - The Slither Wing / Ancient Cormoria encounter (Section 9 — Ancient Cormoria flashback) — Reid + Sable + player are pulled into Ancient Cormoria via Dreamstone resonance
  - **Cormor's Avalon-rest** (Section 9 — Cormor's True Status) — Cormor and Keerin used a Dreamstone to open a path to the distant past where Cormor sleeps in dreamless rest
  - **The Mt. Silver / Future-Red encounter** (Section 7 — Future-Red; Section 9 — Keerin) — Keerin's pact-power transports the player across temporal-cosmological space to face Future-Red
- **Bloodmoon Ursaluna side quest** — used as a catalyst in the Teddiursa-pick post-Champion content
- **The protagonist's sealing ritual** — Osrid (with Ting-Lu) eventually uses the Dreamstone's power to seal the Treasures back at the cost of his memory

**Time flows asymmetrically across Dreamstone displacement** — ~12 subjective years pass in Ancient Cormoria for every ~10 real-world years. Sable Ashland's 12-year subjective stay in Ancient Cormoria during a 10-year present-time absence is the canonical example of this asymmetry.

### The Mega-Dreamstone Shard Quest — Parallel Main Quest `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`

**The single largest structural addition of v0.9.7.** Dreamstone Ruination's narrative spine is now a *double helix:* the public-facing Trial circuit and the clandestine Mega-Dreamstone shard hunt, running in parallel across all 9 chapters of the game leading to the Championship.

**The Mega-Dreamstone — what it was:**

Cormoria once possessed a **Mega-Dreamstone** — one of only **two confirmed Dreamstones in known existence**. The other is the **Dreamstone deeply hidden in the royal palace** in the capital (already-locked v0.9.6 detail; the Beads and Sword Cults suspect this Dreamstone exists but have not located it). **The Mega-Dreamstone and the palace Dreamstone are the only two intact natural Dreamstones documented in Cormorian institutional memory.**

At some point in Cormoria's history (timing `[OPEN]`; cause `[OPEN]` but likely cult-related), **the Mega-Dreamstone was shattered into 9 shards.** The fragments were scattered across Cormoria — some hidden in remote locations, some buried in ancient ruins, some held secretly by Cormorian figures who recognized what they had and chose to safeguard rather than weaponize.

**The quest — Umbra's secret main task for the prince:**

After the Eden theft scene in Act I (Section 6 — The Eden Theft Scene), **Cadmus Umbra briefs Osrid on the clandestine objective:** **recover all 9 shards of the lost Mega-Dreamstone.** This is the **secret parallel main quest** the player undertakes alongside the public-facing Trial circuit.

**Why it must be clandestine:**
- **Hidden from the cults at all costs.** If Baradus or Goma learned the empire was assembling Mega-Dreamstone shards, every shard location would become an immediate cult target. The cults are *desperate* for an intact Dreamstone catalyst (Section 8 — Cult Cosmological Framework; the Tablets and Vessels Cults' Dreamstone *fragments* turned to dust during incarnation rituals, leaving the cults *unable* to repeat the operation without a sufficiently intact Dreamstone). **Nine assembled shards = the cults' single greatest prize and most dangerous tool — if they could acquire even one shard, much less several, the timeline of cult-Treasure-incarnation would accelerate dramatically.**
- **Hidden from the broader Cormorian public.** The empire's official intelligence apparatus is compromised (Silas Moray as Vizier of Commerce controls much of it). A public quest for the shards would leak through cult-aligned officials to Baradus immediately.
- **Hidden even from Rhydia (during the first half of the game).** Rhydia is the public Champion and the focus of cult intelligence-gathering. Compartmentalizing the shard-hunt from her keeps her free of the *operational* knowledge that would make her a target for cult interrogation. **Umbra's broader pattern of compartmentalizing operational knowledge from his beloved pupil** (which causes the Umbra-Rhydia tension at the ~50% reveal — see Section 7 — Cadmus Umbra) extends to the shard-hunt.

**Intelligence source:**

The intelligence on the shard locations comes from **Nemo Korolev's private mercenary counter-intelligence network** (Section 7 — Nemo Korolev). Nemo's network has, over the years he has been Trial Baron, gathered fragmentary intelligence on:
- Where the original Mega-Dreamstone shattering occurred and which directions the fragments dispersed
- Which Cormorian figures have, knowingly or unknowingly, become custodians of individual shards
- Which shards have been lost to ruins, caves, or other geographical concealment
- Which shards are at risk of being discovered by the cults independently

**Nemo provides this intelligence to Umbra through their long-standing personal trust.** Umbra deputizes Osrid as the operational mover. **Nemo does not know Osrid is the prince** during the first half of the game; he knows only that *Umbra has a trusted operative* recovering the shards on the empire's behalf. The compartmentalization preserves the tight 3-person axis (Umbra → Osrid; Nemo → Umbra) without anyone other than Umbra holding full operational visibility.

**Locked shard locations `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`:**

| Shard # | Location | Custodian | Recovery Method |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Ancient cave in the far desert/mountains | **The sleeping Tinkaton Matriarch** (Section 6 — Tinkatink Line; she guards the shard, awakening when the player intrudes deep enough) | Defeat the Matriarch in combat (mid-game side quest) |
| 2 | Held secretly by the Archbishop | **Ambrose Caymen** (Section 7 — Ambrose Caymen; he has held it for years, paralyzed by indecision about whom to entrust it to) | Defeat Ambrose in combat → he surrenders the shard upon gaining clarity through partnership-demonstration |

**Remaining 7 shards: `[OPEN]`** — locations and custodians to be distributed across the 9 chapters of the game in parallel with the Trial circuit progression. **The 7 remaining shards' design will integrate with major story beats:**
- Some shards may be in cult-controlled locations the player penetrates as part of cult-war operations
- Some may be in noble-house custody (knowingly or unknowingly)
- Some may be in ancient ruins or specifically the Ashland Labyrinth, the Jalviss ruins, or Ancient Cormoria
- Some may be in Templar Arceus religious locations (other bishops besides Ambrose may hold shards, or smaller artifacts that the cult-coup-foil reveals as shard-containing)
- **Each shard recovery beat should be a meaningful story moment** — not a fetch quest, but a beat that *reveals character* (Ambrose), *reveals worldbuilding* (the Tinkaton Matriarch), or *resolves a subplot* (cult operation, noble-house intrigue, ancient-ruin exploration)

**Why the shards matter — the cosmological function:**

The reassembled Mega-Dreamstone is *the empire's intact-Dreamstone tool* — usable for the climactic sealing ritual at the Necropolis (Section 10 — Act VI; Section 8 — Cult Cosmological Framework's *three conditions for incarnation/sealing* require Dreamstone catalysis as one of the three conditions). **Without the assembled shards, the climactic sealing ritual may not be possible.** *The shard-hunt is, in cosmological terms, building the tool that ends the game's central crisis.*

**Cross-reference to existing cosmology:** The Dreamstone the player encounters at climactic moments (per the v0.9.6 locked Dreamstone subsection) is *most likely the reassembled Mega-Dreamstone*, recovered shard by shard across the game. **This retroactively explains** why the player has access to a sufficiently powerful Dreamstone for the sealing — the empire's protagonist *assembled* the catalyst across the entire campaign.

**The palace Dreamstone — relationship to the Mega-Dreamstone Shard Quest `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`:**

The Dreamstone hidden in the royal palace remains separate from the shard quest. It is:
- A *separate* intact Dreamstone, distinct from the Mega-Dreamstone
- Hidden in the palace under Rhydia's institutional protection
- Goma's target during the Royal Palace Assassination Plot (Section 10 — Act II) — *Goma was after the palace Dreamstone, not knowing the empire was simultaneously assembling shards of the Mega-Dreamstone elsewhere*
- *Preserved* through Goma's plot's failure

**Two Dreamstone sources, one cosmological mission:**

The palace Dreamstone is *Rhydia's responsibility to protect*; the Mega-Dreamstone shards are *Osrid's responsibility to recover*. **The twin Champions' parallel responsibilities re-enact the brother-sister mirror at the cosmological-resource level** — both are guardians of intact-Dreamstone catalysts, both will eventually contribute their guarded resources to the empire's defense.

### The Four Cursed Objects `[LOCKED]`

- The Sword (becomes Chien-Pao)
- The Tablets (become Wo-Chien)
- The Vessel (becomes Ting-Lu)
- The Beads (become Chi-Yu)
- All four exist in Cormoria in our era. Two of the Treasures (Wo-Chien, Ting-Lu) were incarnated before the game begins; one (Chi-Yu) is partially incarnated and destroyed during gameplay before regenerating; one (Chien-Pao) is not yet incarnated at story start.
- Player gathers all four during the climax, seals the beasts, then carries the four sealed objects east as Veddev.

**The Treasures can regenerate `[LOCKED]`:**

This is a critical worldbuilding fact. The four Treasures of Ruin are cosmic-tier embodiments of negative emotion. Their physical bodies *can be destroyed* under sufficient force (e.g., Koraidon's Breaking Swipe destroys Chi-Yu's body during the Mt. Ceram beat), but **they can regenerate under the right conditions**.

This is why **sealing — not destruction — is the only solution to the cult crisis.**
- Destruction fails: the Treasures reconstitute given time
- Sealing succeeds: the Treasures become *contained* rather than annihilated
- The Paldean king's canonical sealing of the Treasures (per SV lore) is what holds them across the two-millennium gap until DM's era
- **Osrid's sealing ritual at the climax is the originating seal** that the Paldean king inherits and reinforces ~2000 years later

The regeneration mechanic also explains why **Chi-Yu can return as the third Treasure in the final battle** despite its body being destroyed by Koraidon at the 70% mark. The cult-controlled environment of Baradus's final-battle stronghold provides the conditions for re-incarnation.

### Braviary Inter-City Travel System `[LOCKED]`

The empire-era equivalent of Dreamstone Mysteries' Drifblim Pass (Section 11 Principle 4), but operated by the **Templar Arceus order** (Section 4) rather than a private company.

**How it works:**
- **Braviary** is the **national Pokémon of Cormoria** — its public symbol and cultural icon
- Templar Arceus members in every city carry Braviary as their partner Pokémon
- The local Templar at each city's Tavern (Section 4) offers Braviary travel between cities
- Player can pay a **small fee** to the Templar to be flown to another city
- **Fee scales by distance** — closer cities cheaper, distant cities more expensive (realistic empire-era pricing)
- This replaces the modern Fly mechanic in a temporally-appropriate framing

**Acquisition timing:**
- Player gains access **mid-game**, like DM's Drifblim Pass — `[OPEN — specific gating moment]`
- The acquisition is a milestone event — it transforms the player's movement model

**Pokémon type distinction:**
- **Regular Braviary (Unovan): Normal/Flying** — the proud civic-service form. Templars carry this.
- **Hisuian Braviary: Psychic/Flying** — the brutal hunter variant. Less beloved by Cormorian culture.
- **Silas Moray's ace Thauma is a shiny Hisuian Braviary** (Section 7 — Silas). The fact that the corrupted Vizier of Commerce carries the *aberrant* form of the national bird is a visible thematic statement: the corrupted institution has produced its own corrupted patron.
- Custom shiny palette work is planned for Hisuian Braviary specifically (Section 14)

### Silas Moray's Memory-Erasure Mechanics `[LOCKED]`

The psychic mind-influence Silas uses on Pierra (and others) is mechanically constrained:

- **Short-term memory only** — erasures cover hours to days. Cannot erase years, cannot erase core identity memories, cannot erase fundamental sense of self.
- **Requires reinforcement** — erasures fade under sufficient emotional reinforcement (strong emotional events, contradictory evidence). This is why Silas must maintain Pierra's manipulation *daily*, not set-and-forget. It is sustained psychic labor.
- **Why Silas doesn't erase Osrid's memory on first encounter:** the limitation makes such a quick erasure cost-prohibitive (Osrid would re-encounter contradictions immediately, fragmenting the erasure). Only sustained presence and ongoing reinforcement enables long-term mind-control.
- **In-game implications:** this constraint prevents memory-erasure from becoming a plot-convenience tool. Silas cannot just erase the player's progress or "reset" the narrative.

Full description in Section 2 (Mega Evolution policy in Cormoria). Brief mechanical summary:
- Possession of Mega Stones is registered with the state
- Deployment requires state sanction

- **Osrid's Baxcaliburite gift is a coronation symbol** (Section 6) — Frigibax-pickers receive it post-unmasking as a political acknowledgement of his returned authority
- Unauthorized Mega Evolution is a grave offense legally and culturally
- The Cormorian cultural attitude toward Megas is *grave* due to the Jalviss disaster

### Behemoth Bash (Tinkatink-line Cormorian variant) — Custom Move `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`

A custom move designed specifically for the Tinkatink starter line, replacing the canonical Zamazenta signature move's data with a Cormorian variant that defines the Tinkatink line's signature offensive identity.

**Stats:**
- **Type:** Normal · **Category:** Physical · **Power:** 100 · **Accuracy:** 95% · **PP:** 10
- **Learner:** Tinkatuff at Lv 30 (with Tinkatink → Tinkatuff evolving at Lv 24 canonically, so the move first becomes accessible on the mid-stage); Tinkaton inherits it via standard evolutionary learnset persistence

**Custom property — Steel-effectiveness override `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`:**

Behemoth Bash deals **super-effective damage to Steel-type Pokemon regardless of the move's resolved type after type-changing abilities such as Pixilate.** This is a *move property*, not a *type-chart override* — the effectiveness multiplier vs. Steel is hardcoded into the move's damage calculation, independent of whatever type the move resolves to.

**Pixilate interaction — the line's signature offensive identity:**

- **Base move:** Normal physical, 100 BP, super-effective vs. Steel (custom property)
- **With Pixilate active (Hidden Ability):** Normal → Fairy, ×1.2 power multiplier → 120 effective BP Fairy move
- **Against Dragon targets:** Fairy is 2× into Dragon → **240 BP effective damage vs. Dragon**
- **Against Steel targets:** Steel-effectiveness override fires regardless of Fairy retyping → 2× × 120 BP = **240 BP effective damage vs. Steel**
- **Against neutral targets:** 120 BP Fairy-typed STAB hit (with Tinkaton's 85 Atk this is a respectable but not overwhelming damage profile)

**Result:** *The only move in the entire game that hits both Dragon-types and Steel-types for super-effective damage.* Combined with the Tinkatink line's defensive bulk (Tinkaton 125/125 Def/SpD, Battle Armor crit-immunity option, HP 75) and Pixilate's STAB boost, the Pixilate + Behemoth Bash build can survive most threats and KO the two type-categories most resistant to ordinary offense. **The Tinkatink line becomes the *dragon-killer and steel-killer specialist* of the starter trio** — mechanically the perfect expression of its bloodline heritage (Section 6 — Tinkatink Line Dragon-Slayer Bloodline).

**Thematic resonance:**

The line's mechanical kit is *retroactively perfect* for its bloodline lore. Pixilate (locked v0.9.2, before the v0.9.7 dragon-slayer-clan lore was developed) is canonically *the Fairy-retyping ability designed for dragon-slaying*; Behemoth Bash (v0.9.7) extends that dragon-killer profile to *also* cover Steel. **The retroactive alignment is so clean it reads as if Pixilate was always meant for this line** — when in fact the design choice (Pixilate as Tinkatink HA) preceded the bloodline lore by several patch versions. **This is one of the project's happiest retroactive lore-mechanics resonances.**

**Engineering implementation:**

- **Zamazenta does not appear in Dreamstone Ruination** — not in any encounter table, cinematic, or cult roster. The implementation approach is to *overwrite* the canonical Behemoth Bash entry rather than create a new move. **Same MOVE_BEHEMOTH_BASH constant**, modified power/accuracy/PP/effect to match the Tinkatink-line variant, modified learnset assignment to remove from Zamazenta (irrelevant) and add to the Tinkatink line (Lv 30 on Tinkatuff/Tinkaton).
- **The Steel-effectiveness override requires custom engine support** — a new MOVE_EFFECT flag (or equivalent) that the damage calculator checks: *"if move has Steel-effectiveness-override property, multiply final damage by 2× when target is Steel-type."* This is the only engine-level new code for the move; the rest is data modification.
- **Engineering effort:** moderate. Approximately 30-40 lines of code: move data modification (~5 lines), new MOVE_EFFECT flag (~10 lines including enum + handler), damage calculator integration (~15 lines), learnset assignment (~5 lines), localization (~5 lines).
- See Section 14 — Production Discipline for full engineering tracking.

### Blood Moon (Ground) — Custom Type Modification `[LOCKED v0.9.2]`

**The canonical move Blood Moon is retyped from Normal to Ground in Dreamstone Ruination.** All other properties of the move are preserved.

**Specification:**
- **Type:** Ground (changed from canonical Normal)
- **Category:** Special
- **Base Power:** 140
- **Accuracy:** 100%
- **PP:** 5
- **Effect:** Cannot be used twice in a row consecutively (canonical Blood Moon restriction retained)
- **Distribution:** Bloodmoon Ursaluna (Blue Moon) only — learned via the Master Tutor at level 45+ as the evolution gating move for the Cormorian Blue Moon variant (Section 6 — Trial 6 Master Tutor Evolution Gates)

**Why the retype:**

Ground-type Blood Moon is the **strongest Ground special move in the game by a wide margin.** Canonical Ground specials top out at Earth Power (90 BP) and Sandsear Storm (100 BP); there is no canonical 140 BP Ground special. By retyping Blood Moon to Ground, Ruination establishes Bloodmoon Ursaluna (Blue Moon) as the *definitive Ground-type special attacker* in the project — uniquely so, with no canonical Pokemon matching its niche.

This retype is *the* defining mechanical feature of the Blue Moon Teddiursa line, paralleling Mega Baxcalibur's role for Frigibax-pickers and Divine Cuirass's role for Tinkatink-pickers.

**Mechanical context within the Blue Moon line:**

With Bloodmoon Ursaluna (Blue Moon)'s 135 effective Sp.Atk + STAB bonus + Ground-type Blood Moon at 140 BP, the effective power on a Blood Moon strike is **devastating**. Combined with:
- Soul-Heart (or Lunacy post-Peat-Block) Sp.Atk snowballing across the battle
- Calm Mind setup option (+1 Sp.Atk per use)
- Hyper Voice + Boomburst + Hyper Beam as Normal-special STAB alternatives that don't trigger the "no consecutive Blood Moon" restriction
- Mind's Eye removing the Ghost-type wall against Normal STAB

The line's combat output approaches or exceeds the trio's other two apex forms in late-game scenarios where it can set up and snowball.

**Implementation:** Modify existing MOVE_BLOOD_MOON entry to change type from `TYPE_NORMAL` to `TYPE_GROUND`. All other move data is unchanged.

### Lunacy — Custom Ability for Bloodmoon Ursaluna (Blue Moon) `[LOCKED v0.9.2]`

**A new custom ability designed for Dreamstone Ruination.** Special-attack version of Supreme Overlord (canonical Kingambit ability).

**Acquisition:** Post-Champion. Bloodmoon Ursaluna (Blue Moon) consumes the Ancient Peat Block consumable item, which permanently replaces the Pokemon's current ability with Lunacy. **One-way transformation** — the prior ability is lost.

**Ability mechanics — two-axis snowball:**

1. **Passive at switch-in (Speed scaling from fainted allies):**
   - When Bloodmoon Ursaluna (Blue Moon) is sent into battle, its Speed receives a multiplicative boost based on the number of fainted allies on its team.
   - **Suggested implementation:** +10% Speed per fainted ally, max +50% at 5 fainted allies.
   - Mirrors Supreme Overlord's "boost from allies' deaths" mechanic, but applied to **Speed** instead of Attack/Special Attack.

2. **Reactive on KO (Sp.Atk stage boost):**
   - Each time Bloodmoon Ursaluna (Blue Moon) KOs an opponent, its Sp.Atk receives **+1 stat stage** (stacks with existing stages up to the +6 cap).
   - Mirrors Soul-Heart's mechanic, but triggers only on the user's own KOs (not allies').

**Strategic role:**

Lunacy is a **Champion-finisher ability**. Bloodmoon Ursaluna (Blue Moon) with Lunacy is designed to be the *last* Pokemon on the field, sweeping the opponent's remaining team after the player's earlier Pokemon have fallen. The longer the battle goes, the stronger the user becomes:

- Each ally faint = +10% Speed (up to +50%)
- Each opponent KO = +1 Sp.Atk stage
- Combined with Blood Moon (Ground, 140 BP), Moonblast, Hyper Beam, Calm Mind, Slack Off — **this is endgame sweeper mechanics taken to their logical maximum**

**Why this ability exists thematically:**

The Blue Moon Teddiursa line is the trio's *special-offense apex*. Lunacy is the *coronation-tier ability transformation* that completes the line's identity as the project's strongest Sp.Atk snowball threat. The mechanism (consuming a Cormorian-lore artifact to replace the ability permanently) mirrors the project's broader thesis that *partnership grows over time* — the bond with Bloodmoon Ursaluna deepens through post-Champion content and culminates in a literal transformation of the Pokemon's nature.

**Implementation:**
- New ABILITY_LUNACY entry
- Speed-multiplier-on-switch-in logic (parsing `gPlayerPartyCount` for fainted allies)
- +1 Sp.Atk stage on KO logic (extending existing on-KO hooks similar to Soul-Heart implementation)
- Compatible with stat-stage cap rules

`[OPEN]` Final stat-boost numerics — current spec (10% Speed per fainted ally / max 50%; +1 Sp.Atk stage per KO) is the proposed default; user can adjust if balance testing demands

### Crabominable Adoption Mechanic `[LOCKED]`

Custom event triggered by the player's mid-game victory over Baradus (Section 8 — Baradus profile). Full mechanical specification:

- **Trigger:** Player wins the mid-game 6v6 fight against Baradus. Baradus discards Crabominable in fanatical rage.
- **Choice:** Player can approach Crabominable and offer food/drink to befriend it (optional).
- **Adoption outcome (if befriended):**
  - Crabominable joins the party as a **shiny**
  - **Perfect IVs** across all stats
  - **EV optimization:** 252 Attack / 129 Defense / 129 Special Defense (510 total — at cap)
    - Minor implementation note: 129 EVs in Def/SpDef wastes 1 EV per stat (EVs round down by 4). Alternative implementation: 252/128/128 = 508 total, leaving 2 EVs the player can re-allocate. Functionally identical at the stat-point level.
  - **Held item:** Punching Glove (canonical SV item, boosts punching moves by 10%)
  - **Player customization:**
    - **Nature** — player choice
    - **Ability** — player choice among: **Slush Rush** (weather-synergy), **Iron Fist** (universal punch-power), **Defiant** (hidden ability — +2 Attack when any stat is lowered)
    - **Nickname** — player choice
- **Final-battle dialogue triggers (if Crabominable in party):**
  - Baradus pre-battle dialogue mocks the adoption as "refrigerated leftovers" and escalates threats to Pierra and Rhydia
  - Specific KO dialogue fires if Crabominable specifically defeats **Chien-Pao** or **Beartic (Shardik)**
  - Small additional acknowledgement if Crabominable lands the final blow on Baradus
- **Thematic significance:** The IV/EV mechanic encodes the project's thesis at cellular level. Crabominable's response to the player's compassion physically maximizes its biological potential — potential Baradus failed to bring out because he didn't bond. **Partnership produces what engineering cannot.** Baradus's bred Beartic (Tough Claws) is at engineered maximum; the player's adopted Crabominable is at *natural* maximum through bond. They are competitive at the level of raw stats.

**Implementation effort estimate:** ~4-6 hours of focused implementation: custom event script (~50-100 lines), nickname/nature/ability selector UI integration, party-add event, flag-checks for final battle, conditional dialogue variants. See Section 14 for engineering checklist.
