### Justice — Rhydia's Royal Heirloom Iron Valiant `[LOCKED]`

The combat-mechanical specification for Justice, Rhydia's permanent ace (Section 7 — Rhydia). In-world identity, mythological framing, and acquisition history are documented in Section 2 — Justice subsection.

**Species and stats:**
- **Iron Valiant** (Fairy/Fighting Paradox Pokemon)
- Shiny variant
- Base stats: 74/130/90/120/60/116 (BST 590)

**Ability:** **Sharpness** — boosts slicing moves by 50%

**Moveset (all four moves are slicing moves; all are Sharpness-boosted):**
- **Sacred Sword** — Fighting, 90 BP, ignores stat changes. Sharpness-boosted to ~135 effective BP.
- **Tachyon Cutter** — Steel, 50 BP × 2 hits (Gen 9 signature multi-hit; always hits, never misses). Sharpness-boosted to ~75 BP × 2 = 150 combined effective BP.
- **Bitter Blade** — Fire, 90 BP, heals 50% of damage dealt. Sharpness-boosted to ~135 effective BP plus recovery. Provides Justice's self-sustain.
- **Mighty Cleave** — Rock, 95 BP, ignores screens/Protect. Sharpness-boosted to ~142 effective BP, *unblockable* — Justice's anti-stall move.

**Item:** Choice Scarf (1.5× Speed) — Iron Valiant's base 116 Speed × 1.5 = 174 effective Speed, outspeeding nearly every Pokemon in the game.

**Strategic profile:**
- **Type coverage:** Fighting / Steel / Fire / Rock hits virtually every defensive typing in the game for at least neutral, with super-effective coverage on Dark, Normal, Ice, Rock, Steel, Fairy, Bug, Flying, Grass, Ghost, Psychic, Ghost, and Ice. **There is no clean type-counter to Justice** — even Fairy/Steel (a typing that resists Dragon and Dark) eats super-effective Steel STAB or Fire damage.
- **Choice Scarf lock:** Justice locks into one move per switch-in. The player can attempt to predict the move and switch optimally, but Iron Valiant's Speed + the four-move coverage means almost any switch-in eats a Sharpness-boosted slicing move.
- **Self-sustain via Bitter Blade:** Justice can hit-and-recover, making it durable against retaliation. The combination of high offensive pressure + self-healing is difficult to outlast.
- **Anti-stall via Mighty Cleave:** Players who try to set up screens, Protect, or Substitute tactics find Mighty Cleave bypasses them. Justice cannot be wall-stalled.

**Combat tier:** Justice is the strongest known Pokemon currently bonded to a trainer in all of Cormoria. It is the project's *most powerful regular boss-fight asset* (the Treasures of Ruin and Mythical Pokemon excepted). **The Champion fight where the player faces Justice should be one of the hardest battles in the game.**

### Puff — Rhydia's Childhood Drampa `[LOCKED]`

The combat-mechanical specification for Puff, Rhydia's second permanent ace and childhood starter (Section 7 — Rhydia).

**Species:** Drampa (shiny lineage variant — generationally bred within the Cormoria royal family)

**Base stats:** 78/60/85/135/91/36 (BST 485). Drampa is a Special attacker with strong bulk on the special side, modest physical defense, and very low Speed.

**Ability:** **Multiscale** — halves damage taken at full HP. Rare on Drampa (Hidden Ability); the combination of shiny + Multiscale + the elite moveset makes Puff a *generationally bred* Pokemon.

**Moveset:**
- **Dragon Energy** — Dragon, 150 BP scales with HP percentage (full HP = full power; reduced HP = reduced power). **Most powerful Dragon STAB in the game when at full HP.** Multiscale + full HP + 150 BP Dragon Energy is the design centerpiece of Puff's kit.
- **Hyper Beam** — Normal, 150 BP, recharge turn. Coverage move; canonical Drampa learnset. Combined with Multiscale's free first turn, it is a reasonable opening-turn nuke.
- **Thunderclap** — Electric, 70 BP, +1 priority (attack-attack-only priority). **Ruination-added learnset modification — Drampa does not canonically learn Thunderclap; Ruination adds it to Puff's learnset as a unique inheritance move.** Lets Puff strike priority against fast attackers.
- **Flash Cannon** — Steel, 80 BP, canonical Drampa learnset. Coverage for Fairy types that resist Dragon Energy.

**Item:** Assault Vest (+50% Sp.Def, prevents all status moves). Puff is purely offensive.

**Strategic profile:**
- **Multiscale + Assault Vest opening:** Puff comes in at full HP, takes half damage from any attack thanks to Multiscale, has +50% Special Defense from Assault Vest. **Puff's first turn on the field is essentially a free turn against most opponents.** During that turn it fires a 150 BP Dragon Energy.
- **After taking damage, Dragon Energy weakens proportionally.** So Puff's design centerpiece is *spending its first-turn opportunity well*. Players must choose whether to absorb Puff's opening nuke and bring counter-pressure, or to sacrifice a Pokemon to weaken Multiscale and degrade Puff's threat.
- **No status moves:** Assault Vest prevents Puff from using status. Puff is purely an offensive piece. **Rhydia has no defensive support from Puff** — her team's defensive utility comes from the cycling members.
- **Coverage gaps:** Puff's four moves are Dragon, Normal, Electric, Steel. *Steel-types* resist both Dragon and Normal and don't take SE from Electric (some) — meaning Steel/Fairy or Steel/Ghost defensive walls can potentially trade with Puff. Players who plan ahead can counter Puff with specific Steel-typing.

**Combat tier:** Despite Drampa's modest BST 485, Puff functions as **the strongest known Dragon under a trainer's command in the whole Empire** due to Multiscale + Assault Vest + Dragon Energy + the inherited moveset. Puff is *generationally* bred and *strategically* equipped to punch far above its base species' weight.

**Personal/family significance:** Puff is Rhydia's *childhood starter* — the bond goes back to before her father's death. **Puff is the longest-running relationship in Rhydia's life** (20 years). Puff knew Osrid as a child and would presumably recognize him by scent or familiarity when they meet again in the Champion fight. *Puff carries the family's lost childhood into a fight against the returned brother* — a moment of immense thematic weight if the writing exploits it (Section 7 — Rhydia).

### Leyline Resonance — Mechanical Summary `[LOCKED — cross-reference Section 2]`

The full worldbuilding for Leyline Resonance is in Section 2. Mechanical implications for gameplay:

**The Cormorian region has unusually thin barriers between space and time.** Specific points across the geography have *measurably* higher resonance — Leyline points. These dictate:

- **Where Paradox Pokemon naturally appear** — Cormoria's Paradox Pokemon density (Justice, Domdaniel, Slither Wing, Gouging Fire, Koraidon) is a direct consequence
- **Where Treasures of Ruin can be incarnated** — each Treasure has a *specific* compatible Leyline point (Section 8 — Cult Cosmological Framework)
- **Where the climactic sealing ritual must take place** — the sealing uses the same cosmological machinery the cults use, in reverse

**Locked Leyline points in Cormoria:**
- **Mt. Ceram lava pool room** → Chi-Yu's Leyline point (Ashland family unknowingly guards)
- **Distortion World gate within Mirroh Necropolis** → Chien-Pao's Leyline point (House Langerin guards)
- **Hoya River area** → Wo-Chien's Leyline point (likely an ancient altar; site of the Tablets Cult's successful incarnation before Baradus slaughtered them)
- **A deep forgotten cave system** → Ting-Lu's Leyline point (site of the Vessels Cult's successful incarnation before Nemo intervened); specific geography `[OPEN]`

### The Dreamstone in the Royal Palace `[LOCKED]`

One of the Dreamstones in Cormoria's possession — a powerful resonance object kept in the imperial palace. **This is the Dreamstone Goma's Beads Cult targeted in the early-game assassination plot** (Section 8 — Goma; Section 10 — Story Spine).

The Dreamstone in the royal palace is significant for several reasons:
- It is *needed* for the Beads Cult's intended Chi-Yu incarnation ritual (resonance-based, requires a sufficiently powerful Dreamstone amplifier)
- Its existence is broadly known (Beads Cult intelligence has located it; Goma plans an operation around it) but its specific palace location is presumably secured
- The royal family has access to it as part of their imperial inheritance
- `[OPEN]` Whether the Dreamstone surfaces in later gameplay (e.g., the climax sealing ritual using this specific stone, or whether the climax uses a different Dreamstone)

The Beads Cult's failure to obtain this Dreamstone is what forces Goma's escalation to the Mt. Ceram lava-pool operation (Section 8 — Goma) — they need a Dreamstone, and if they cannot take the palace one, they must find another resonance pathway.

### Dreamstones as Catalysts — The Arceus-Avoidance Mechanism `[LOCKED — cross-reference Section 8]`

Dreamstones are *accumulations of millions of years of memories and emotional energy*. Their role in the cult cosmological framework (Section 8 — Cult Cosmological Framework) is precise:

**Why Dreamstones are the *perfect* catalyst for Treasure incarnation:**
- Their energy is *consumable* and *finite* — they amplify resonance without lasting cosmic disruption
- They provide the precise *emotional-energy signature* needed to bridge the Leyline-resonance gap during incarnation
- Their use does NOT produce the universe-shattering side effects that crude incarnation methods would cause
- Therefore, **Dreamstone-catalyzed incarnation does not draw Arceus's intervention.** This is the entire reason Baradus and Goma pursue them — they are the *only* known catalyst that allows the cults to incarnate Treasures without triggering cosmic retribution.

**The Dreamstone-fragment failure that derailed the Tablets and Vessels Cults `[LOCKED]`:**

Per Section 8 — Cult Cosmological Framework: **the Tablets and Vessels Cults both used Dreamstone *fragments* for their incarnation rituals**, and those fragments turned to dust during the ritual. The fragments were one-use, single-incarnation catalysts that were consumed in the process.

This means:
- Baradus, after slaughtering the Tablets Cult and seizing Wo-Chien, **got no usable Dreamstone catalyst from the operation**
- Goma got nothing from the Vessels Cult's success — Nemo took both Ting-Lu and whatever remained
- Baradus and Goma have been searching for *intact, sufficiently powerful* Dreamstones for the past 10+ years
- **The cults move slowly because they need rare catalysts, and the catalysts they could find are already destroyed.**

**Implications for sealing:** The climactic sealing ritual at the end of Act VI uses Dreamstone catalysis *in reverse* — instead of using a Dreamstone to *incarnate* a Treasure, the player + Ting-Lu use it to *contain* all four Treasures. **The cosmological framework supports both directions.** The cults' meticulous research into Dreamstone-catalysis is what eventually enables the seal that ends them. They built the toolkit; the player uses it against them.

### Phancero — The Pre-Pokemon Cosmic Shadow `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

**Cosmological scope:** Phancero is **Arceus's shadow-self made manifest** — the accumulation of Arceus's negative emotions and "flawed" desires/instincts across millions of years of cosmic time, which eventually gained its own sentience. Phancero is *not* an external threat to Arceus's creation; **Phancero is Arceus's own self-rejected darkness made manifest.**

**Per Section 2 — Cormorian founding myth, the full Phancero origin story:**
- Accumulated as Arceus's shadow across millions of years pre-creation
- Gained sentience and developed envy of human-Pokemon partnership bonds
- Slipped a fragment of itself into the material world off the coast of Cinnabar Island — what modern Pokemon canon calls **Missingno** (the canonical glitch is Phancero's first material incursion)
- Grew through worship into a glitchy ghostly fourth legendary bird alongside Articuno/Zapdos/Moltres in early Kanto
- Fed on memories and emotional energy worldwide; eventually decimated the Celestica nomadic tribes that Cormor came from
- **Erased from the material world** by Cormor + Keerin's pact through the four-objects channeling ritual
- **Persists in the void outside space and time** in true form

**Current status (present-day story):**

- **Phancero is *erased*, not sealed.** There is no seal to break.
- Phancero's true form persists in the void where the entity originally accumulated as Arceus's shadow
- The entity *can still detect* events in the material world via cosmic resonance — particularly, it would detect the full incarnation of all four Treasures of Ruin as a homing-channel signal
- Full Treasure incarnation could provide Phancero with a *new pathway* to push more of itself back into material reality (potentially via Distortion gate hijack)
- **Arceus's intervention threshold is reached if Phancero begins to re-emerge** — and Arceus's intervention at cosmic scale would likely *wipe out creation entirely* (Section 8 — Cult Cosmological Framework)

### Keerin — The First Pokemon, Cosmic-Tier Companion `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

**Cosmological scope:** Keerin is **the first Pokemon ever created by Arceus** — originally as Arceus's companion to alleviate the creator-god's loneliness, later reassigned as observer-and-extension of Arceus's will across creation.

**Per Section 2 — Cormorian founding myth and the new Keerin lore lock:**

- **Created by Arceus as her first creation** — predates Mew, predates the creation trio (Dialga/Palkia/Giratina), predates all other Pokemon
- **Original purpose:** Arceus's companion. **Even the supreme creator-god experienced loneliness; Keerin was the answer.**
- **Reassigned by Arceus** after humanity and Pokemon were created — Arceus wanted *the being he trusted most* to observe the world and serve as his extension when intervention was truly necessary
- **Intervention authority constraint:** Keerin acts only when crises *exceed the scope of free will of created beings* (the free-will principle is Arceus's foundational ethical framework). Phancero qualified; ordinary mortal conflicts do not.
- **Pact with Cormor** is the first authorized intervention. **Cosmologically extraordinary** — the firstborn of creation extending herself across the divine-mortal gap to a single chosen mortal.

**Keerin is female** (she/her).

**Keerin's relational hierarchy:**

Keerin only truly loves two beings in existence: **Arceus** (her creator, the being she was created for) and **Cormor** (the human she chose to make a pact with). **That is the entirety of her direct love across cosmic history.**

She loves **Osrid to a lesser extent because of his heritage** — Osrid is Cormor's direct male-line descendant, the closest thing to Cormor still walking in the world. **Keerin's love for Cormor extends through bloodline as a distant echo** that reaches Osrid as a recognized-favor.

**Pokemon data — full specification `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`:**

| Attribute | Value | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Typing** | Normal/Ghost | Normal = Arceus's default-creation type; Ghost = the cosmic boundary-crosser type |
| **BST** | ~720 | Matches Arceus exactly; "second only to Arceus" is *relational hierarchy*, not statistical |
| **Stat distribution** | `[OPEN]` | Suggested: 120 HP / 100 Atk / 130 Def / 130 SpA / 130 SpD / 110 Spe for cosmic-observer defensive profile, OR 120/120/120/120/120/120 to mirror Arceus exactly |
| **Ability (Paragon mode)** | **Wonder Guard** | Immune to all attacks except super-effective. Combined with Normal/Ghost = effectively invincible to all but Dark. |
| **Ability (Elite mode)** | **Opportunist** | Copies any stat boost the opponent gains. Strong competitive ability; not near-invincibility. |
| **Type matchups** | Only weakness: Dark (×2 from Ghost). Immune to: Fighting, Ghost, Normal. | The single Dark vulnerability is *cosmologically calibrated* — Dark is precisely the typing of the four corrupted Treasures of Ruin |

**The Wonder Guard mechanic is the project's mechanical apex.** Combined with Normal/Ghost typing, Keerin in Paragon mode is **immune to all attacks except Dark-type** — including Mega Rayquaza's Dragon Ascent, Mewtwo's most powerful psychic moves, Eternatus's signature attacks. The ONLY thing that can hurt her is Dark. This is *cosmologically resonant* — her vulnerability is precisely targeted to the cosmic threat she was reawakened to fight.

**Once Keerin is captured, she can only be used during the post-Champion final act content** — the Necropolis Assault and the 7v9 final battle against Baradus. **Keerin cannot be used during pre-Champion content, nor during the post-Champion Ancient Cormoria starter-bonus quests.** This is enforced by quest-gate logic. Lore-wise: Keerin's mandate is *cosmic-tier crisis intervention only*; she does not battle for the player in ordinary contexts.

**Capture mechanism — the Mt. Silver / Future-Red sidequest:**

See Section 7 — Future-Red entry for the full sidequest specification. Summary:
- Player reaches Keerin in a chamber `[OPEN]` (specific location)
- Keerin tells the player they must prove worthy *as Cormor was* by defeating the strongest trainer of the future
- Player is transported to Mt. Silver summit; faces Future-Red with six broken legendaries
- Player allowed Keerin + 2 reserve Pokemon (3v6 disadvantage)
- Win condition: defeat all six legendaries
- Post-victory: Future-Red speaks ("...so this is the power of the past. Interesting."); player blacks out; returns to original chamber; **Keerin joins the player's party**

Keerin has been provided as user-generated sprite content in the asset library:

- `KEERIN - Shiny Front.png` / `KEERIN - Shiny Back.png` / `KEERIN - Shiny Icon.png` (shiny palette: pale pink + white)

After Cormoria's eventual collapse, the line presumably ceases to exist anywhere in the modern Pokemon world *unless* descendants of the Umbra family (eventually Professor Tenebris of DM era) preserved seed-stock. `[OPEN]` whether this is canonical to the project.

### The Book of the Moon — Lost-History Lore Reward `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

A 10-11 chapter readable book that constitutes the project's deepest cosmological lore reward. **Available only through chapter-collection across the game + compilation by the Blue Moon Hermit in post-game content.**

**Origin and content:**

The Book of the Moon is the Blue Moon Hermit's (Sage Lethys of the Celestica's) lifework — his centuries-long act of grief-preservation, recording the lost histories of the pre-Cormorian nomadic tribes (including Cormor + Keerin's pact, Phancero's nature, the four objects' inversion into the Treasures of Ruin, and the cosmological framework of the project's deepest layer).

**Per Section 7 — Blue Moon Hermit entry:** The Hermit *is* essentially the last carrier of the pre-Cormorian nomadic tradition. The Book is the *single most comprehensive lore-document* in the project's worldbuilding.

**Chapter content distribution `[OPEN]`** — proposed 10-11 chapter structure:

1. **Chapter 1:** Pre-Pokemon universe / Arceus and Keerin's creation
2. **Chapter 2:** Arceus's negative-emotion accumulation / Phancero's emergence in the void
3. **Chapter 3:** Phancero's Missingno-incursion off Cinnabar Island; early Kanto worship; the Four-Birds-pantheon
4. **Chapter 4:** Phancero's bird-form era; devouring memories worldwide; growing power
5. **Chapter 5:** Phancero's arrival in the unnamed nomadic region (proto-Cormoria); the decimation of nomadic tribes
6. **Chapter 6:** Cormor's tribe's destruction; Cormor's grief and quest for vengeance; Lethys's role as Sage and brother
7. **Chapter 7:** Cormor meets Keerin; the formation of the pact; the nature of the partnership
8. **Chapter 8:** The four objects (tablets, sword, vessel, beads) and the channeling ritual; Roma's miko-consecration role; Cormor's blacksmithing role
9. **Chapter 9:** Phancero's erasing; the residual contamination; the four objects' inversion
10. **Chapter 10:** Cormor's post-erasing exploration; the uncovering of Justice / Iron Valiant; the founding of Cormoria
11. **Chapter 11 (Hermit's notes):** The Hermit's synthesis — the inverted-Dreamstone framework, the modern cult cosmology, the warning about full Treasure incarnation re-opening Phancero's pathway, the partnership thesis as Cormoria's founding act

`[OPEN]` Whether Lethys and Roma's romantic-coupling history and the Cormor-sleeping-or-dead question are included in the Book or remain implied through in-game dialogue only (cleanest design is **the latter** — the Book covers history through Cormor's empire-building era; the personal aftermath remains for player inference)

**Chapter collection mechanic `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`:**

- **10-11 chapter fragment items** scattered across Cormoria at locations of historical/cosmological significance
- **Chapter pickups are *missable*** — players can complete the game without finding any
- **Each chapter is readable when found** — provides immediate partial-context lore
- **`[OPEN]` Specific location placements for each chapter** — should be tied to thematically-resonant locations:
  - Chapters about Phancero's pre-Cormorian era → ancient ruins, libraries, Templar Arceus sites
  - Chapters about Cormor's pact → places connected to Cormor's history (royal palace archives, ancient tribal lands)
  - Chapters about the four objects → near the Hoya River altar, Vessels-Cult cave, Mt. Ceram, the future Necropolis area
  - Hermit's-notes chapter → the Hermit's hermitage itself, obtained only after the Hermit compiles

**Hermit compilation:**

When the player has collected the chapters and engages with the Blue Moon Hermit:

- **For Teddiursa-pickers:** compilation happens automatically after the Ancient Peat Block quest resolves (the Hermit is already with them); the compiled book is given as part of that quest's conclusion
- **For Frigibax / Tinkatink-pickers:** compilation requires a separate post-game visit to the Hermit's hermitage; the player must reach the Hermit and present the chapters (or have collected them; the visit triggers compilation if the chapter-collection flag is set)
- **The Hermit adds annotations and contextual notes** — his own synthesis of the chapters into a coherent cosmological narrative
- **The compiled book is added to the player's Key Items inventory** as a readable book object

**Reading the compiled book:**

- The book is interactable from the Key Items menu; selecting it opens a multi-page reading interface
- Player can read all chapters + the Hermit's annotations
- Reading is optional and can be done at any pace
- The book persists across saves

**Design intent:**

> *"It's an interesting lore reward for players who want to know 'the big picture' and every little detail. Basically a reward for nerds before they go and confront the Swords Of Ruin once and for all at the Necropolis."*

Engaged players ("nerds") enter the climactic Necropolis Assault with the full cosmological context. **The depth scales with the player's engagement; the project's full cosmological depth is gift, not gate.**

**Implementation:**
- Custom Key Item: "Book of the Moon" (compiled)
- 10-11 Custom Key Items: "Book of the Moon Chapter [N]" (individual fragments)
- Item placement events scattered across the game world
- Hermit compilation event with chapter-count check
- Readable book UI (multi-page interface)
- Text content authoring for all 10-11 chapters + Hermit annotations (substantial worldbuilding-text work)

### Pact-Extended Longevity — The Three-Tier Framework `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

The mechanical/cosmological framework that explains the multiple long-lived characters in the project. **Per Section 2 — Cormorian founding myth and Section 7 — Madame Roma:**

**Tier 1 — Cormor himself:**

- **True immortality with no drawbacks** — the most extreme cosmic blessing in Pokemon history
- Hidden from his closest companions (Cormor never told Lethys or Roma; see Section 9 — Cormor's True Status below)
- Unique in Pokemon history
- Still effectively present in some sleeping/dormant form `[OPEN]` per Cormor's True Status

**Tier 2 — Cormor's inner circle (cosmic-tier longevity):**

- **Lethys / Blue Moon Hermit** — Celestica baseline (already long-lived as tribal heritage) + Keerin pact-blessing through blood-relation and proximity to Cormor. **Easily thousands of years.** Still alive in present-day game (~2000 years post-founding).
- **Madame Roma / Roma Langerin** — Not Celestica by birth, but pact-blessed via *instrumental role in the erasing ritual* (miko consecration of the four objects) AND received the *original doubly-blessed Langerin name* (Arceus + Keerin double-blessing). **Centuries to potentially thousands of years.** Still alive in present-day game.
- **Cogita** (canonical PLA character; Lethys's niece) — Celestica baseline + pact-blessing-by-blood-relation-to-Lethys (one degree removed from Cormor). Long-lived; still alive in canonical PLA era (~2500 years post-founding). See Section 12 — Cogita cross-canon connection.

**Tier 3 — Later Cormorian noble houses (standard longevity):**

- Founders of later noble houses (House Moray, House Ashland, House Surge, House Raptora, House Alban, House Jalviss): received *Arceus-blessing only* (the Keerin-component had diminished across centuries of surname-grantings)
- **Standard human lifespan with vitality blessings**
- None of them are alive in present-day Cormoria
- Their bloodlines continue but no longer carry pact-blessed longevity

**The mechanism:**

Cormor's pact with Keerin extended his lifespan substantially — but Keerin's blessing also *cascaded* outward to those who shared a deep bond with Cormor by proximity or blood:
- Direct partnership-in-pact-mission proximity (Lethys, Roma) → cosmic-tier extension
- Blood relation to Cormor through Lethys (Cogita) → cosmic-tier extension
- First Arceus-blessed surname recipient (Roma) → additional doubly-blessed extension
- Later surname recipients → progressively diminished extension (Keerin component fades over time)

**This is internally consistent mechanism design.** One foundational mechanism (the Keerin pact's cascading blessing) with *layered amplifications* (Celestica heritage, blood-relation degree, first-recipient doubled-blessing) explains the precise distribution of long-lived characters across canonical Pokemon canon.

**Implications for Kimaris and modern House Langerin:**

Kimaris Langerin (Section 7 — Kimaris) has **standard mortal lifespan** despite being a descendant of two Tier 2 ancestors (Lethys and Roma). The pact-blessing did NOT pass to subsequent generations of House Langerin — only the *original* recipients (Lethys, Roma, Cogita) carry the cosmic-tier longevity. Their descendants inherit *powers* (Marshadow generational ace, Distortion gate guardianship) but not longevity.

`[OPEN]` Whether any other Cormorian characters from the pre-Cormor era are still alive but uncatalogued (likely none beyond the three Tier-2 characters)
`[OPEN]` Whether the longevity mechanism is ever explicitly explained to the player in-game (most likely via the Book of the Moon's Hermit annotations chapter)

### Cormor's True Status — Immortality and the Avalon Sleep `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

The deepest character secret in Cormoria's cosmological history — known with certainty only to Keerin, suspected by Lethys and Roma, unknown to everyone else.

#### Cormor's actual nature `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

**Cormor is *immortal*** — true, unbridled immortality with no drawbacks. Keerin granted this to Cormor as the pact's primary effect.

**Critical detail: Cormor did NOT tell Lethys or Roma about his immortality.** Keerin told *Cormor* the full extent of the pact's effects; Cormor chose to keep this from his closest companions across the entire pact-era and the empire's two centuries of building.

**Why hide it?**
- Possibly because Cormor knew Lethys and Roma would worry about him being condemned to outlive everyone he loved
- Possibly because Cormor himself didn't yet know how he felt about it and didn't want to share an unprocessed reality
- Possibly because revealing it would have *changed* his relationships with them — they would have seen him as fundamentally different from themselves
- *This was Cormor's first secret from his closest people.* A man who had spent his pre-pact life in open partnership with his brother-friend Lethys began keeping the deepest truth about himself private — *because the cosmic weight was too much to share.*

**Lethys and Roma never learned the truth in life.** Even across centuries living together, they understood Cormor as long-lived (like themselves) rather than immortal-with-no-drawbacks. **They still don't know in the present day.** Their late-game hints about Cormor "possibly sleeping" are based on *suspicion* born of long friendship, not knowledge of his true cosmic status.

#### The three centuries of weariness and the Avalon sleep `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

**Cormor became weariest of life by the time he had ruled for three centuries.** Three hundred years of empire-building, three hundred years of seeing companions die, three hundred years of carrying the cosmic weight of having saved reality. **The most blessed man in Pokemon history became the most tired.**

**This is the project's deepest character-tragedy thread.** Immortality without drawbacks is itself the drawback — Cormor was given a gift no other human ever received, and the gift slowly *exhausted his spirit*.

**The Dreamstone time-path resolution:**

Cormor and Keerin found a Dreamstone and used it to **open a path to the distant past.** This establishes Dreamstones as time-displacement tools (Section 9 — Dreamstone, updated above). Cormor found himself a place where he could **fall into a dreamless sleep** and rest his weary soul **until Keerin believes the time is ready to awaken him.**

**Design elements:**
- **Dreamless sleep** — not unconscious, not deathlike, but *true rest*. Cormor's consciousness is quieted entirely; he does not dream.
- **Keerin is the awakener** — *not* a hero, *not* a crisis-detection mechanism, *not* Arceus's intervention. **Keerin alone decides when Cormor wakes.** This is the partnership-thesis at its most cosmologically pure — *the partner-Pokemon judges the trainer's readiness to act.*
- **Whether the time ever comes is *never known* by design** — the question is permanently open

#### The Avalon / Schrödinger framing `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

This is **a permanent open question by design**. The story does NOT resolve it.

**Inspirations:**
- **King Arthur and Avalon** — the mythological pattern of the once-and-future king resting in a sacred place until a future cosmic-need calls him back
- **Schrödinger's Cat** — the quantum-cosmological framework of an entity simultaneously being and not-being until observed

**Synthesis:**
- **Avalon-pattern:** Cormor is the sleeping founder-king awaiting cosmic need
- **Schrödinger-pattern:** Cormor is simultaneously *dead* and *alive* — neither status confirmed; both possible at once until a future observer collapses the wave function

**Why the cult crisis does NOT wake Cormor:**

The cult crisis (the Treasures of Ruin / Sword Cult Necropolis Assault / 7v9 final battle) is **resolved by Osrid**, Cormor's only living male-line direct descendant in the present day. **A crisis that would require the greatest hero of all time to re-awaken has not materialized.**

> "Since Osrid manages to resolve the catastrophe with the Treasures Of Ruin, a crisis that would require the greatest hero of all time to re-awaken has not materialized yet. It would have to be a crisis beyond all other crises to justify his awakening."

This is the *gorgeous* generational-narrative completion: **Cormor doesn't need to wake because his descendant has proven worthy of his legacy.** The partnership-thesis re-enacts itself across generations — Cormor + Keerin saved the world from Phancero through partnership; Osrid + his starter + Ting-Lu save Cormoria from the Treasures of Ruin through partnership. **The grandson does not need the grandfather to wake because the grandson has inherited the grandfather's capacity for partnership-tier accomplishment.**

#### Lethys's and Roma's late-game hints `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

**These are character-level lore-delivery beats — *implied*, not explicit.**

**Lethys (the Blue Moon Hermit)** uses specific verbal cadences when referring to Cormor:
- Present-perfect verbs implying continued existence: *"he rests"* rather than *"he died"*
- Framings that suggest interrupted-not-ended state: *"he completed his work and stepped away"* rather than *"he passed"*
- Players who pay attention will sense the implication without it being stated

**Madame Roma (Roma Langerin)** carries similar verbal patterns — *centuries* of conversation with Cormor have given her phrasings that imply continued engagement, not cessation. Her hints derive from her two-centuries co-architect partnership with Cormor rather than Lethys's deep-brother bond — *different angle, same implication*.

**Together they form a complementary pair of attentive observers** who suspect — but cannot confirm — the truth of their friend's fate. Players who hear both sets of hints reach the strongest possible inference that Cormor sleeps rather than dies.

`[OPEN]` Specific dialogue lines for Lethys and Roma — should be carefully designed to maintain ambiguity while planting recognizable patterns
`[OPEN]` Whether either or both characters share an on-screen scene where they exchange these hints together (the cleanest design: yes, briefly, in late-game post-Necropolis content)
`[OPEN]` Cormor's specific resting location — `[OPEN]` permanently by design. Possibilities:
- A hidden chamber within the Mirroh Necropolis (connecting his rest to House Langerin's guardianship)
- A hidden grove somewhere in pre-Hisuian region (eventually canonical PLA-era Cogita's grove?)
- Within Ancient Cormoria's temporally-displaced zone (the player visited part of this region during the Mt. Ceram beat)
- Somewhere lost to history — even Lethys and Roma don't know exactly
- *Multiple hints scattered across the game, none definitive*

### The Fossil Revival System — Madame Roma's Quest Mechanic `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

**Mechanic specifications:**

- **Multiple fossil discovery events scattered across the player's journey** through Cormoria — `[OPEN]` specific locations
- **Each fossil discovery → trip to Madame Roma → revival → Pokemon added to player's available roster**

The female Rock-type Vizier (the v0.9.7-locked unnamed Rock-master Vizier, Section 7 — Female Rock Vizier entry) has a **shiny Tyrantrum** as her ace — *well-known for its regal power in Cormoria*. When the Vizier was just a girl, she found a Tyrunt fossil and Madame Roma revived it for her. **The shiny Tyrantrum is therefore one of the small handful of Roma's revivals.**

This implies:
- The Rock-type Vizier's lifelong-bonded ace was *created* by Madame Roma's gift
- Her Vizier status traces partially to this girlhood revival event
- She is one of Madame Roma's *recognized worthy recipients*

### The Difficulty Modes and Mechanical Frameworks `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`

Three player-chosen difficulty modes plus toggleable Nuzlocke and Randomizer options. This is the project's mechanical-design framework that allows different audiences to engage with the project at their preferred challenge level.

**Mode selection occurs at game start** (before character creation), and the chosen mode applies to the entire playthrough. **The player cannot change modes mid-game** — once chosen, the mode locks for that save file.

#### Classic Mode — The Casual Tier

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| **Battle style** | Switch / Set toggleable; player choice |
| **Level caps** | None — player can over-level if they grind |
| **Battle items** | Full canonical Pokemon item economy; unlimited use during battle |
| **Enemy difficulty** | Fixed difficulty curve — enemies do not scale with player |
| **Enemy IV/EV spreads** | Competitive (trainers still tuned, but not max IVs) |
| **Keerin sidequest** | **NOT available** — Classic players cannot do the Mt. Silver Future-Red quest |
| **Nuzlocke options** | NOT available |

**Design intent:** Casual player. Wants to experience the story. Wants meaningful but not punishing combat. **The "experience the game" tier.**

The exclusion of Keerin from Classic is intentional. Keerin is *not* a casual reward; she is the *cosmic-tier apex* and only players who choose meaningful difficulty earn the opportunity to face the Future-Red test.

#### Elite Mode — The Standard Hardcore Tier

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| **Battle style** | Set mode only — no switching to swap during turn opportunities |
| **Level caps** | Moderate — the Trial Baron's highest-level Pokemon always *matches* the player's current level cap |
| **Battle items** | **Only one item per battle** (or one item per round in multi-stage battles unless story specifies otherwise) |
| **Enemy IV spreads** | **Maximum IVs (31 across all stats)** for every enemy trainer Pokemon |
| **Enemy EV spreads** | **Scaling EVs** — gradually increasing as the player progresses; mid-game opponents have partial EVs, late-game opponents have full EVs |
| **Keerin sidequest** | **Available** with **Opportunist** ability (not Wonder Guard) |
| **Nuzlocke options** | **Toggleable from in-game menu** |
| **Starter Nuzlocke exemption** | **The starter cannot be permanently removed** even under Nuzlocke; KO'd starter does not "die" |

**Design intent:** Players who want a meaningful challenge but don't need maximum-difficulty cosmic-tier ceiling. **The "play seriously" tier.** Most engaged players who want a real challenge will choose Elite.

**The Keerin Opportunist mechanic:** Opportunist (canonical Pokemon ability from Scarlet/Violet) copies any stat boost the opponent gains. This is *thematically resonant* — Keerin observes and adapts. It's also *significantly less broken than Wonder Guard* — strong competitive ability but not near-invincibility.

#### Paragon Mode — The Hardcore Competitive Tier

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| **Battle style** | Set mode only |
| **Level caps** | **Harsh** — the two strongest Pokemon of every Trial Baron, Vizier, and major boss fight is always **5 levels higher** than the player's level cap |
| **Battle items** | One item per battle (same as Elite) |
| **Enemy IVs** | Maximum (31 across all stats) |
| **Enemy EVs** | **Optimized max** (252 in primary stat / 252 in secondary stat / 4 in tertiary stat — the canonical EV-optimization pattern) for every Pokemon in addition to maxed IVs |
| **Enemy movesets** | **Perfectly optimized** — no filler moves; every slot is competitive |
| **Enemy AI** | **Especially ruthless** — possibly using maximum-difficulty AI patterns (predictive switching, optimal move selection, item-use timing) |
| **Player starter IVs/EVs** | **Perfect IVs and EV spread from the very beginning** — no grinding required |
| **Keerin sidequest** | **Available** with full **Wonder Guard** + perfect IVs + perfect EV spread |
| **Nuzlocke options** | Available (same toggleable system as Elite); starter exempt |

**Design intent:** Hardcore competitive players. Extreme-difficulty enthusiasts. **The "test yourself against the project's maximum" tier.** Recommended only for players who specifically seek brutal challenge.

**The Wonder Guard Keerin** is the *full* cosmic-tier reward — only obtainable through completing the Mt. Silver Future-Red test in Paragon mode. **Paragon players who beat Future-Red receive the most powerful Pokemon entity in any project.**

#### The Nuzlocke Toggle System

**Nuzlocke is a *toggleable* option** rather than a separate mode. Players in Elite or Paragon can opt into Nuzlocke rules and toggle them off later if they want a less brutal experience for any segment.

**Standard Nuzlocke rules supported (`[OPEN]` confirm specific rules):**
- KO'd Pokemon are released / permanently removed from the player's team (the "death" rule)
- Only the first encounter in each route can be caught (the "first encounter" rule)
- Optional: nickname-everything rule (purely cosmetic)
- Optional: species clause (cannot catch a Pokemon of a species already in your party)

**The starter exemption:**

**The starter is *exempt* from Nuzlocke "death."** Even with Nuzlocke active, a KO'd starter does not get permanently removed from the team. The starter's cosmic narrative significance is preserved under all play modes. **The starter cannot be Nuzlocke-permanently-deleted.**

This integrates with the unboxable-starter mechanic (Section 6) — the starter is structurally protected from both PC deposit and Nuzlocke removal.

#### The Randomizer System

**Randomizer option available** to players who want randomized Pokemon. Many configurable settings:
- `[OPEN]` Specific randomizer options — likely include: randomize wild encounters, randomize trainer teams, randomize abilities, randomize movesets, randomize hold items
- `[OPEN]` Compatibility with Nuzlocke options + difficulty modes (likely all are independent toggles)

**Starter exclusion:**

**The starter and its evolutions are NOT randomized regardless of randomizer settings.** Frigibax → Baxcalibur, Teddiursa (Blue Moon) → Bloodmoon Ursaluna (Blue Moon), and Tinkatink → Tinkaton remain the only three possible starter selections under any randomization configuration. The starter's cosmic narrative significance is preserved under all play modes.

#### Mode-locked content gates

| Content | Classic | Elite | Paragon |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main story | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| All Trial Baron and Vizier fights | ✓ | ✓ (capped) | ✓ (harsh cap) |
| Hall of Fame coronation | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Post-Champion Ancient Cormoria starter-bonus quests | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Book of the Moon chapter collection | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| Book of the Moon Hermit compilation | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |
| **Keerin sidequest (Mt. Silver Future-Red)** | ✗ | ✓ (Opportunist) | ✓ (Wonder Guard) |
| 7v9 final Necropolis battle | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ |

#### Implementation

Substantial engineering work — see Section 14 for full task specifications. Key components:
- Mode selection UI at game start (before character creation)
- Mode flag tracked in save data
- Difficulty modifiers applied across the game based on mode flag (level cap enforcement, IV/EV settings, item-use restrictions, AI behavior)
- Keerin Pokemon data variant — Wonder Guard version (Paragon) and Opportunist version (Elite)
- Nuzlocke logic system — toggle menu, death-tracking, party-removal-on-faint, starter-exemption rule
- Randomizer settings menu, randomization tables with starter-exclusion logic
- Mode-locked content gates (Keerin sidequest gating based on mode flag)

## 10. Story Spine

### Act structure `[LOCKED — corrected climax architecture]`

This act structure reflects the corrected climax: **Osrid becomes Champion + Regent-King BEFORE the final cult confrontation**, not after. The trainer-merit path culminates with Rhydia. The cult crisis is then addressed by Osrid in his *political* capacity as Regent-King, not just as a trainer.

**Act I — Homecoming and First Steps**
- Rainhawk returns to Cormoria from the Kanto-Johto-Hoenn war
- Reunion with Rhydia (who knows him), distant interactions with Glaive (who doesn't recognize him through the mask)
- Umbra's gift scene — starter Pokémon choice (Frigibax / Teddiursa / Tinkatink)
- **The Eden Theft Scene `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`:** Eden breaks into the basement of Umbra's estate and steals **the starter mechanically strongest against Osrid's choice** (Silver/Crystal homage; Section 6 — Eden Theft Scene). Type-counter logic: Frigibax pick → Tinkatink stolen; Teddiursa pick → Frigibax stolen; Tinkatink pick → Teddiursa stolen. **The unstolen third starter stays with Umbra**, to be deployed in the Act V Vizier gauntlet on Umbra's team. **Eden keeps the stolen starter through the entire game including the post-game marriage to Veddev in Paldea.** This is the inciting moment of Eden's deuteragonist arc (Section 7 — Eden).
- **Umbra briefs Osrid on the Mega-Dreamstone Shard Quest `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`** — the clandestine parallel main quest (Section 9 — The Mega-Dreamstone Shard Quest). Intelligence sourced from Nemo Korolev's counter-intelligence network (compartmentalized — Nemo doesn't know Osrid is the prince at this point). **Hidden from the cults and from the broader Cormorian public.** The briefing is conditional on the Eden theft having just happened — Umbra waits because the theft confirms cult intelligence has noticed Osrid's existence, providing both heightened-stakes context and operational cover.
- **Conditional return-to-lab scene if Osrid picked Tinkatink `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`:** During a follow-up visit to Umbra's lab, Umbra briefly tells Osrid the Tinkatink's heritage — the egg was taken from the deep-desert clan descended from the sleeping ancient Tinkaton Matriarch, "stolen under their noses." Section 6 — Tinkatink Line; Section 7 — Cadmus Umbra. Umbra does not dwell on the moral weight of the theft.
- Discovery that the cults have been spreading false news of his death
- Discovery that two of the four cults are already destroyed (Tablets by Baradus, Vessels by an unknown "Usurper") — the cult landscape isn't four-cults-active but a more dangerous two-cults-and-mysteries
- Inciting incident pulls him into investigation (`[OPEN]` specifics)
- First Trial: **Kimaris Langerin (Ghost)** — Crowley the Spiritomb test, level cap 23. The player learns the Trial structure is *real Champion-tier combat*, not introductory.
- **First Mega-Dreamstone shard recovery `[OPEN — placement]`**: at some point in Act I or early Act II, the player recovers their first shard. Location and circumstances `[OPEN]`; possibly tied to a low-stakes worldbuilding event that introduces the shard mechanic without overwhelming the player.

**Act II — The Trials and the Two-Cult War**
- Player travels Cormoria, undertaking Trials 2-8. Currently designed: **Trial 2 (Reid Ashland / Fire)**, **Trial 3 (Manus Surge / Electric)**. Trials 4-8 are `[OPEN]`.
- **Trial 3 is thematically loaded for Osrid:** facing Manus Surge means confronting the *paternal source* of his combat mentor Boran's corruption. Manus has been openly aware of Boran's "Pokemon as weapons" philosophy and has tried unsuccessfully to counsel his son. Pre/post-Trial dialogue may include Manus asking after Boran's overseas service.
- Player encounters the **6 recurring stat-themed rivals** at various points (Section 11 Principle 3): HP/Attack/Defense/SpA/SpD/Speed-focused trainers who get progressively stronger
- Player encounters and defeats Sword Cult and Beads Cult operatives in escalating engagements
- **Post-Trial 2 — The Goma Royal Palace Assassination Subplot** `[LOCKED — major narrative beat]`:
  - **The first major event that brings Osrid and Rhydia's paths into proximity in the present-day story.** (Specifics of exactly how their paths intersect during this subplot are `[OPEN]` — to be designed.)
  - **Two cult objectives:** (1) Assassinate Rhydia (her growing popularity is a political threat to the cults' creeping influence); (2) seize the **Dreamstone hidden in the royal palace** (needed for Goma's planned Chi-Yu incarnation ritual via resonance)
  - **Player participates in scripted doubles-format battles alongside Rhydia** against low-level Beads Cult acolytes and one **Bishop** (Beads Cult mid-tier rank — see Section 8)
  - **Rhydia leaves Puff and Justice at home** for this engagement, explicitly stating that she only has the lower-level Pokemon she is in the process of fully training for her new team iteration. Player sees portions of her cycling team (Aegislash / Swampert / Zebstrika / Hisuian Lilligant / Stonjourner / Talonflame) but not her two aces.
  - **This is the player-Rhydia cooperative relationship establishment** (Section 7 — Rhydia). They fight on the same side; the player learns to respect her combat capability through partnership before they eventually face off in the Champion battle at the climax of Act V.
  - **The plot fails.** Rhydia survives. The Dreamstone is preserved. **Goma escapes** and is now stained in Cormorian public consciousness as the cultist who attacked the beloved princess.
  - **This failure directly causes Goma's later escalation to the Mt. Ceram operation in Act III** (Section 8 — Goma; see below).
- **The Jordan Ramses & Wakahisa Cross-Purposes Arc `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`** (post-Royal-Palace-failure narrative beat):
  - **Two independently-hired assassins target Osrid simultaneously:**
    - **Baradus contracts Jordan Ramses** (Poison-type, chef-cover; exiled youngest of disgraced House Ramses — Section 7 — Jordan Ramses) — *Baradus does not coordinate this with Goma*
    - **Goma contracts Wakahisa** (Bug-type, ninja-style; foreign-born — Section 7 — Wakahisa) — *Goma does not coordinate this with Baradus*
    - **The cults' chronic disunity (Section 8 — Sword vs. Beads enmity) has resulted in two simultaneous independent assassination contracts on the same target by rival cults.**
  - **The cross-purposes encounter** (dark-humor moment): Jordan and Wakahisa become aware of each other's existence and their respective employers. **Each views the other as a competing contractor whose existence undermines their own contract's success.** They begin **fighting each other to kill each other** rather than fighting Osrid.
  - **Osrid intervenes to prevent their mutual murder.** *The prince becomes the protector of the people sent to kill him.* The player fights both assassins to stop them from killing each other.
  - **Conversion arc — both bond with Osrid:**
    - Treated with the same partnership-ethos Osrid extends to his Pokemon, both assassins are *seen* as people rather than as weapons (the partnership thesis applied to humans)
    - Both abandon their assassin contracts
    - Both decide to pursue the Trials as legitimate trainers
    - Both become recurring friends through the rest of the game
  - **Strategic consequence for the cults:** Both Baradus and Goma waste resources on contracts that not only failed but *converted to assets* for the opposition. A small but real strategic loss for both cults — and another concrete operational expression of cult disunity (Section 8 — cult-disunity as one of Cormoria's protective barriers).
  - **Tonal function:** Provides genuine comic relief in a project register that is otherwise heavily weighted (cosmological horror, family tragedy, character deaths). The moment when two professional killers awkwardly discover they're working at cross-purposes and try to kill each other rather than their actual target is *exactly* the kind of humor the project needs to make its dramatic moments land harder.
  - `[OPEN]` Exact geographic location of the cross-purposes encounter and Osrid-intervention
  - `[OPEN]` Specific dialogue beats — Jordan's chef vocabulary surfaces in his combat patter, Wakahisa's ninja-archetype delivery is contrasted with the dawning recognition of being out-classed
- **The Mid-Game Tinkatink Clan Side Quest `[LOCKED v0.9.7 — structural lock]`** (Act II or III, placement `[OPEN]`):
  - Player travels to the far desert / mountain region (specific naming `[OPEN]`) where the Tinkatink dragon-slayer clan dwells
  - **The quest has more thematic weight if the player travels with an Arctibax or Tinkatuff in their party** (Section 6 — Tinkatink Line). Arctibax = dragon-lineage tension; Tinkatuff = stolen-child-returning tension; Teddiursa/Bloodmoon-Ursaring = neutral encounter
  - Combat structure: multiple Tinkatinks → Tinkatuffs → **the ancient Tinkaton Matriarch awakens** when the player intrudes deep enough into her cave
  - **The Matriarch guards one of the 9 Mega-Dreamstone shards** (Section 9 — The Mega-Dreamstone Shard Quest). Defeating her is the *only* way to recover this shard.
  - **The Matriarch's awakening is the project's small-scale rehearsal of the larger Cormor-might-wake possibility** (Section 9 — Cormor's True Status / Avalon Sleep). Pokemon-cosmological-tier longevity, parallel-but-distinct from the Cormor-Lethys-Roma-Cogita pact-extended-longevity framework.
  - Post-quest: the Matriarch returns to her cave to sleep again; the clan continues to defend their territory; **Osrid is one of the only outsiders to have entered the clan's domain and survived**
  - `[OPEN]` Matriarch's name, clan name, desert/mountain region naming
  - `[OPEN]` Matriarch's combat profile (level cap, exact moveset, abilities — likely above-cap encounter precedent established by Roaring Moon/Walking Wake at level 120)
  - `[OPEN]` Tinkatink-picker player special dialogue with the Matriarch acknowledging the stolen-egg heritage
- **~50% through the story: The joint Sword Cult encounter with Rhydia.** Up until this point, **only Cadmus Umbra has known that Osrid is the prince *and* has returned to Cormoria** — Rhydia knows her brother is alive (Umbra told her years ago) but believes him still overseas. Not Pierra. Not Glaive. Rhydia joins Osrid for a Sword Cult engagement (specifics `[OPEN]`); during combat the truth is revealed to her — *"Rainhawk Alban" is her brother who has been back in Cormoria for months.* **From this moment onward, Rhydia is the second person who knows Osrid's identity.** She is also the only person who calls him by his first name (the previously-locked detail now has its activation moment).
- **The Umbra-Rhydia tension after this reveal** (Section 7 — Cadmus Umbra). Rhydia realizes Umbra has been withholding her brother's return from her for months. *Umbra weighed his loyalty to the empire's strategic safety against his love for his pupil and chose the strategic calculus.* This complicates their relationship.
- Eden's defection and shift from antagonist to ally
- Glaive interactions and the mid-game prince-reveal to Glaive (Section 7 — Glaive arc point 8) — happens *after* the Rhydia reveal
- Glaive's Shelgon → Salamence evolution (off-screen between scenes)
- **Second Baradus encounter (mid-game, 6v6)** — player wins, Baradus discards Crabominable in rage, **Crabominable adoption mechanic triggers** (Section 9). Player gains shiny Crabominable with the IV/EV/ability customization.
- `[OPEN]` Geographic location of this second Baradus encounter
- `[OPEN]` Geographic location of the joint Sword Cult encounter with Rhydia
- `[OPEN]` Specific Sword Cult plot beat that requires Rhydia's intervention
- `[OPEN]` Order of remaining Trials encountered

**Act III — The Mt. Ceram Pivot (~70% through)**
- **Goma's escalation:** Having failed at the palace assassination/Dreamstone seizure plot, Goma escalates to a more desperate strategy — invade Mt. Ceram, use the lava-pool room deep in the Ashland Labyrinth as the resonance chamber, and revive Chi-Yu through that environment instead of through the palace Dreamstone.
- **The Beads Cult attempts to seize Mt. Ceram** for the lava-pool revival of Chi-Yu through Dreamstone resonance
- Player and **Reid Ashland (2nd Trial Baron, Fire)** attempt to repel the raid
- Beads Cult partially revives Chi-Yu before being stopped
- **Dreamstone resonance goes out of control. Player + Reid + partially-revived Chi-Yu are sucked into Ancient Cormoria.**
- In Ancient Cormoria:
  - Chi-Yu rampages into a **shiny Slither Wing's cave lair**
  - The Pokémon fight; player has the option to capture or defeat Slither Wing during the chaos (single window, unique catchable shiny)
  - **Sable Ashland appears** with his hatched-and-raised **Gouging Fire**, breaking up the Chi-Yu/Slither-Wing fight (Section 7 — Sable). 12 subjective years of survival in Ancient Cormoria have made him a hardened warrior.
  - **Sable swears undying loyalty to Osrid** — he intuits the prince's identity from Eldred-similar bearing without confronting him about it
  - Chi-Yu flees to the ancient temple where Koraidon lies dormant
  - **Chi-Yu accidentally awakens Koraidon, who destroys Chi-Yu's physical body with a single Breaking Swipe**
  - Player party seizes the **singed Beads of Ruin**, escapes via second Dreamstone formation back to present near Mt. Ceram
- **Sable resumes leadership of House Ashland.** The 10-year Ashland treason suspicion is vindicated.
- **Reid Ashland retires from Trial Baron seat** to study under Vizier Umbra (Section 7 — Reid)
- Player now has the singed Beads of Ruin — first Treasure secured by the player's side
- **Harbinger Goma escapes** after this failure; she becomes a desperate antagonist for the rest of the story
- **The Ambrose Caymen Arc `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`** (Act III closing / Act IV opening — placement `[OPEN]` between Mt. Ceram and Trial 9):
  - **Cult-aligned bishops within the Templar Arceus order attempt a coup** — moving to seize the Templar apparatus from Archbishop Ambrose for the Sword Cult's benefit, which would deliver empire-wide religious-civic infrastructure (Tavern network, Braviary travel, institutional moral authority, intelligence-gathering) to Baradus
  - **Osrid intervenes and foils the coup**, fighting off the cult-aligned bishops in a sequence of escalating Templar combat encounters
  - **At that moment, Archbishop Ambrose Caymen** — who has been paralyzed for years by the three-way tension between Silas Moray's manipulations, his good-faith relationship with Umbra, and his deep formative friendship with Nemo Korolev (Section 7 — Ambrose Caymen) — **decides he needs to fight Osrid himself** as the way to *"get to the truth of things"*
  - **The Archbishop fight `[LOCKED v0.9.7]`:** Player vs. Ambrose with Temperance (shiny Kingambit) + Brunhilda (shiny female Perrserker) + rest of his Steel-tier roster. Combat tier appropriate to second-half-of-game placement, between Trial mid-tier and Vizier upper-tier; exact level cap `[OPEN]`.
  - **Resolution — clarity through combat:** Once Osrid defeats Ambrose, **the partnership demonstrated in combat communicates a moral truth that political argument could not.** Ambrose gains clarity. He:
    - **Surrenders the Mega-Dreamstone shard he has been secretly holding** for years (Section 9 — The Mega-Dreamstone Shard Quest; the second locked shard recovery beat)
    - **Begins the process of cleansing cult influence out of the Templar order** — purging the corrupted bishops, restoring the order's institutional health for the rest of the game
    - **Becomes one of Osrid's staunchest allies** through the final half of the game
  - **The trio of redemption-through-combat arcs:** Ambrose joins Silas (Act V Vizier defeat) and Glaive (Act V Vizier defeat) as the third major institutional figure whose moral arc resolves through partnership-thesis combat-demonstration. **All three arcs use the same mechanism.** Section 11 — Design Principles documents this pattern as a v0.9.7 design principle.
  - `[OPEN]` Exact placement within Act III closing / Act IV opening
  - `[OPEN]` Specific dialogue beats for Ambrose's pre-fight, post-fight clarity, and subsequent ally relationship with Osrid
  - `[OPEN]` Whether Ambrose's discovery of Silas's lie about Nemo (the "punishment by the crown" lie — Section 7 — Ambrose Caymen) happens during this arc, or is deferred to Act V/VI after Silas's death

**Act IV — Trials Conclusion, the Sylphon & Stone Exposure, and the Usurper Revealed**
- **The Sylphon & Stone Cult-Funding Exposure beat `[LOCKED]`** — early in Act IV:
  - Following Mt. Ceram's events (Act III), an investigation thread surfaces evidence that the Beads Cult's funding has been routed through Sylphon & Stone, Cormoria's largest merchant guild (Section 2 — Sylphon & Stone)
  - `[OPEN]` Who specifically conducts the investigation — Rhydia's intelligence network, Eden using her defection-era knowledge, Nemo Korolev's pre-Trial-9 intel, or a combination
  - The exposure is publicly disclosed (presumably through the Concordance, possibly through a Templar Arceus order pronouncement) causing **major political fallout**:
    - **Beads Cult loses its primary funding pipeline** — Goma is forced to operate on residual resources for the remainder of the story, increasing her desperation
    - **The Concordance is shaken** — Sylphon & Stone-affiliated members lose legitimacy; some seats may be vacated or contested
    - **Silas Moray is implicated by association** — his Vizier-of-Commerce role oversaw the guild; he is partially responsible for not catching the cult-funding pipeline. This adds pressure on him leading into his Vizier defeat in Act V.
    - **Amber-Letz benefits commercially** — Vandras's family picks up market share as Sylphon & Stone is investigated and partially dismantled. *Two thousand years later, this commercial shift is part of why the Berlitz family becomes dominant.*
  - `[OPEN]` Specific scripted-event structure — investigation sequence, public revelation moment, political-fallout scenes
- Player completes Trials 7 and 8 (remaining Trial Barons `[OPEN]`)
- **Trial 9: Nemo Korolev (Ground)** — the climactic Trial
  - Phase 1: Manticore (shiny Adaptability Gliscor) + standard Ground-type team
  - **Phase 2: Ting-Lu is brought out**, revealing Nemo as **the Usurper** who killed Harbinger Alaric and dismantled the Vessels of Ruin cult
  - **Nemo recognizes Osrid during the battle** through his Eldred-similar battling style. The mutual recognition happens silently, in the same moment as the player realizes Nemo's secret.
  - Player wins the Trial
- **Baradus and Goma raid Nemo's location.** Baradus runs the operation himself, keeping it secret from Silas (who served beside Nemo under Eldred and would be emotionally compromised). They take Nemo and the weakened Ting-Lu.
- **Player fails to stop the kidnapping.** This is the pivotal event of the story — the moment Osrid commits to the political path.

**Act V — The Meritocratic Gate, the Necropolis Assault, and the Unmasking**
- Osrid commits to claiming Champion + Regent-King — using the empire's full institutional authority for the cult confrontation
- **Paragon Gauntlet** — Osrid fights 5 of the 6 recurring stat-themed rivals (Section 11 Principle 3) in succession, no breaks/healing, one healing item allowed. Tests his readiness for the final political tier.
- **4 Viziers in succession `[UPDATED v0.9.7]`** — order reflects the v0.9.7 lock:
  - **The female Rock-master Vizier** (Vizier of `[OPEN portfolio]`) — **first fight**. Her shiny Tyrantrum (Roma's revival from her girlhood Tyrunt fossil) is the ace; rest of team `[OPEN]`. **The Vizier gauntlet's opening fight establishes the elevated combat tier the player will sustain through four consecutive Vizier-level battles.** See Section 7 — Female Rock Vizier; Section 4 — Viziers.
  - **Cadmus Umbra** (Vizier of Education) — **second fight**. Water-type team featuring his signature Jousteel (Water/Steel) ace. **Umbra's team includes the unstolen-by-Eden third starter** from Act I (the starter Osrid did not pick and Eden did not steal — see Section 6 — The Eden Theft Scene). This is the *only* in-game deployment of that third starter; Umbra has been raising it across the entire game as a parallel partner to Osrid's chosen and Eden's stolen. **All three starters are present in the Vizier gauntlet's progression:** player's pick in the player's party, Eden's pick on Eden's team (if Eden assists or fights elsewhere in this period), Umbra's pick on Umbra's team. **No starter is wasted; all three contribute to the story's climactic political-progression arc.** `[OPEN]` exact Jousteel kit and full team composition.
  - **Silas Moray** (Vizier of Commerce) — **third fight**. Thauma (shiny Hisuian Braviary, Psychic/Flying) and Malamar (Dark/Psychic) as aces. **Boss fight with thematic-redemption stakes.** **After Silas's defeat: Osrid's identity is verbally revealed to Silas.** Silas's reaction-dialogue *implies* his pseudo-parental recognition without naming it (audience reads it, no character says it). Osrid/Umbra then reveal the Baradus-killed-Eldred truth. **Silas pledges true loyalty to Osrid.** **Osrid still wears the mask through this scene** — the empire at large has not yet seen his face. **Silas now witnesses the prince fighting Glaive next, then Rhydia, before any unmasking** — his final loyalty is *tested by watching* rather than just declared. *Silas's redemption-as-final-act in the sealing cataclysm is foreshadowed by this witnessed-final-loyalty sequence.* `[OPEN]` Whether Silas verbally tells Glaive (in advance of the Glaive fight) that he has defected, or whether Glaive learns by inference from the cult's sudden operational disorientation.
  - **Glaive** (Vizier of War) — **fourth fight**. Black-and-crimson shiny Salamence (Blackthorn) and the egg-group dragon roster (Sandaconda, Gyarados, Turtonator, Sceptile, Heliolisk, Scrafty). **Glaive's heart-toward-peace turn happens after this defeat (Section 7 — Glaive).** *Now the climactic Vizier moment*, as the final Vizier fought before the Champion — Glaive defeated through partnership-thesis combat *is* the partnership-thesis-moment that the gauntlet builds toward. **The final pre-Champion beat is the partnership thesis fully realized**: the dragon-master whose Salamence evolution was earned through opening his heart is now defeated through *partnership played against him in turn.* `[OPEN]` Whether Glaive's defeat scene includes acknowledgment of Silas's recent defection, which would land differently for him than for Silas (Glaive is loyal, not corrupted; for him the partnership-defeat resolves *vengeance*, not *manipulation*).

**The thematic shape of the v0.9.7 reordered gauntlet:**
- **Fight 1 (Rock Vizier):** Establishes the elevated combat tier — a senior Vizier fight with no special narrative loading; the player's signal that they have crossed from Trial-tier into Vizier-tier
- **Fight 2 (Umbra):** Faces Osrid's longest-trusted ally, who carries the third starter — the *first* Vizier with deep prior relationship to Osrid is fought to claim institutional eligibility for the throne. *Umbra is testing his pupil one final time.*
- **Fight 3 (Silas):** Corruption is purged. Silas redeems. The cult's high-society 2IC defects. *The political layer is resolved.*
- **Fight 4 (Glaive):** Partnership is reaffirmed. Glaive's heart fully opens. The Vizier of War turns toward peace. *The partnership-thesis layer is resolved.*

**Sequence resolves three thematic layers (corruption / partnership / family) in succession:** Silas's defeat purges corruption → Glaive's defeat reaffirms partnership → Rhydia's Champion battle (next major beat) reconciles family. **The climactic ascent is a tightly-structured emotional progression**, each beat clearing one of the project's central thematic concerns before the next can land.
- **Rhydia — the Champion Battle.** The final formal trainer-tier gate. **Team locked (Section 7 — Rhydia, Section 9 — Justice and Puff):** Justice (shiny Iron Valiant with Sharpness, Choice Scarf, four slicing moves) + Puff (shiny Drampa with Multiscale, Assault Vest, Dragon Energy / Hyper Beam / Thunderclap / Flash Cannon) + 4 cycling members selected from Aegislash, Swampert, Zebstrika, Hisuian Lilligant, Stonjourner, Talonflame (Rhydia scouts the player and adjusts her cycling team in advance). Osrid wins. The empire formally acknowledges Osrid as the new Champion.
- **The Hall of Fame Unmasking Sequence (climax of Act V) `[LOCKED]`:**
  1. Osrid and Rhydia enter the **Hall of Fame** — the empire-era proto-Pokemon League Hall of Fame
  2. Osrid's team is preserved with **daguerreotype-equivalent pre-industrial photographic technology** — the image is then placed in a sealed vault to eternally preserve his victory. **Osrid himself is captured in the photograph still masked.** The eternal record shows Rainhawk Alban — *the masked Champion*. The photograph becomes a museum artifact in the same moment Osrid is about to shed the mask itself.
  3. Osrid leaves the Hall of Fame with Rhydia and emerges to a **massive public crowd** waiting outside
  4. **Osrid removes the mask in front of the crowd.** Public, maximally visible. The crowd sees a face they don't recognize — a slightly weathered young man with mild lining around his features (the faded scarring).
  5. **Rhydia announces his true identity** — speaking the player's chosen true name to Cormoria. The crowd realizes they have been celebrating the missing prince.
  6. Rhydia names him **Regent-King and Champion of Cormoria** — transferring both her political authority and her battle authority to him.
- **Eden, present at the unmasking, sees Osrid's face for the first time.** Her experience of this moment is doubled — recognition of the prince she had served + seeing the face of the man she loved. Two reveals collapsing into one.
- **The Necropolis Assault — climactic late-game playable beat `[LOCKED]`:**
  - **`[OPEN]` exact placement** — see Act VI structure below (the Necropolis Assault is now positioned as the opening of the unified Act VI climactic operation, not a standalone repulsion beat)
  - **The Sword Cult assaults the Mirroh Necropolis to use the hidden Distortion World gate as Chien-Pao's Leyline-resonance incarnation point** (Section 2 — Leyline Resonance; Section 8 — Cult Cosmological Framework). **Baradus has discovered the gate's existence late in Act V** (Section 8 — Baradus's late-game discovery beat) and pivots the cult to attack as the climactic operation.
  - Baradus directs the assault personally — he has brought Nemo to the Necropolis for the binding ritual
  - **Kimaris Langerin deploys Marshadow** in defense — the empire signals existential crisis (Section 7 — Kimaris)
  - **Player joins the defense** as the central playable beat of Act VI
  - Multiple Cormorian institutions converge on the Necropolis: **Archbishop Ambrose Caymen with Temperance and Brunhilda** (post-clarity Templar Arceus commitment); House Langerin retainers; the broader Templar Arceus order operatives; **Trial Baroness Brie Moray with Domdaniel** as part of the multi-house defensive convergence; Kimaris Langerin with Marshadow as the central Mythical-tier deployment
  - **This is NOT a "cult-repelled" beat** — the assault begins the climactic operation; the player + allies fight through outer Necropolis combat, descend into the inner Necropolis, reach Nemo's location near the gate chamber, witness his death, and ultimately face Baradus in the 7v9 final battle at the gate chamber itself
  - **This is the largest team-fight set-piece in the game** — multi-stage scripted battle sequence integrating defensive combat + stronghold penetration + 7v9 final battle + sealing ritual as one continuous operation

**Act VI — The Final Cult Confrontation (Unified Necropolis Operation) `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`**

This is the story's true climax, addressed by Osrid in his political capacity as Champion + Regent-King. **All Act VI main-story beats happen at or around the Mirroh Necropolis.** There is no separate Sword Cult HQ raid; the Necropolis Assault IS the climactic operation.

**Act VI opening:** After the Hall of Fame coronation, the cult war intensifies. Osrid is Champion + Regent-King. **Baradus has discovered the Necropolis Distortion gate is Chien-Pao's Leyline point** (Section 8 — Baradus's late-game discovery; `[OPEN]` exact mechanism, likely Silas Moray's intelligence network indirectly leaking the knowledge as the unknowing-enabler completion of Silas's tragic arc).

The Sword Cult mobilizes their entire force toward the Necropolis. Cormorian forces — House Langerin, Templar Arceus, possibly other houses — mobilize to defend. **The player + Cormorian forces converge to intercept the cult and prevent Chien-Pao's incarnation.**

**Three Optional Post-Champion Engagements (player choice; all three can be completed in any order before committing to the Necropolis):**

Players can take *any combination* of three optional engagements before committing to the Necropolis operation. **Each is independent and rewards different player engagement styles:**

1. **The post-Champion Ancient Cormoria Starter Bonus Side Quest** `[LOCKED v0.9.2]` — starter-specific bonus item:
  - **Frigibax-pickers:** quest to acquire the **Baxcaliburite** in Ancient Cormoria → unlocks Mega Baxcalibur
  - **Teddiursa (Blue Moon)-pickers:** quest to acquire the **Ancient Peat Block** in Ancient Cormoria, accompanied by the Blue Moon Hermit → consume on Bloodmoon Ursaluna to replace current ability with **Lunacy**
  - **Tinkatink-pickers:** quest to acquire the **Divine Cuirass** in Ancient Cormoria → grants Tinkaton coronation-tier offensive and survival boosts
  - All three quests take the player back to the same Ancient Cormoria map area accessed in Act III (Mt. Ceram beat), with distinct quest content per starter
  - **This is the *final visit* to Ancient Cormoria** — after this, the player does not return

2. **The Book of the Moon Compilation** (Section 9 — Book of the Moon):
  - Players who have collected the 10-11 chapter fragments throughout the game can visit the Blue Moon Hermit and have him compile the full book with annotations
  - **For Teddiursa-pickers:** compilation happens automatically as part of the Ancient Peat Block quest's resolution
  - **For Frigibax / Tinkatink-pickers:** requires a separate post-game visit to the Hermit's hermitage
  - Reward: the deepest cosmological lore in the project, with the Hermit's annotations contextualizing what the player is about to do at the Necropolis

3. **The Mt. Silver / Future-Red Keerin Sidequest** `[LOCKED v0.9.4]` — **available only in Elite or Paragon difficulty modes** (Section 9 — Difficulty Modes; Section 7 — Future-Red entry; Section 9 — Keerin entry):
  - Player reaches Keerin in `[OPEN]` (specific chamber location)
  - Keerin tests the player's worthiness by transporting them to the top of Mt. Silver from Kanto (via Dreamstone temporal-cosmological displacement)
  - Player faces **Future-Red** — multiverse-displaced older corrupted version of canonical Red — with **6 broken legendaries** (Mewtwo, Urshifu, Yveltal, Mega Rayquaza, Eternatus, Miraidon)
  - Player allowed Keerin + 2 reserve Pokemon (3v6 disadvantage); fight is winnable through targeted Yveltal-counter strategy
  - **Pre-battle dialogue from Keerin:** *"You must overcome the strongest Trainer of the future in order to have a future of your own. Let us see if you are as worthy as Cormor was."*
  - **Post-victory dialogue from Future-Red:** *"... so this is the power of the past. Interesting."*
  - Player blacks out, returns to the original chamber, **Keerin joins the party**
  - Keerin can ONLY be used during the post-Champion Necropolis operation — *not* during pre-Necropolis content
  - **In Elite mode:** Keerin has Opportunist ability
  - **In Paragon mode:** Keerin has Wonder Guard ability + perfect IVs + perfect EV spread

**The maximal-engagement player path:** A player who completes all three optional engagements arrives at the Necropolis Assault with:
- Their starter at coronation-tier bonus form (Mega Baxcalibur / Bloodmoon Ursaluna with Lunacy / Tinkaton with Divine Cuirass)
- The full cosmological context from the Book of the Moon (knows what Phancero is, what the Treasures of Ruin actually are, what's truly at stake)
- **Keerin in their party** (the cosmic-tier apex Pokemon — virtually invincible in Paragon mode)

**This is one of the most rewarding maximal-engagement player experiences in any Pokemon ROM hack.** Engaged players are *cosmologically equipped* for the climax.

`[OPEN]` Whether the Act VI cult war state progresses while the player is on optional engagements (narrative-cost mechanic) or pauses
`[OPEN]` Whether there's a "point of no return" warning before committing to the Necropolis Assault, giving the player one last chance to complete optional engagements

**The Necropolis Climactic Operation — single unified scripted sequence `[LOCKED v0.9.4]`:**

Once the player commits to the Necropolis, the operation unfolds as a single continuous scripted sequence:

1. **Outer Necropolis combat** — player + Cormorian allies (**Kimaris Langerin with Marshadow at level 100; Trial Baroness Brie Moray with Domdaniel; Archbishop Ambrose Caymen with Temperance + Brunhilda**; House Langerin retainers; Templar Arceus operatives) fight through Sword Cult forces in the outer Necropolis grounds. Multi-stage battle encounters with cult Acolytes, Bishops, and possibly named cult lieutenants.

2. **Inner Necropolis descent** — player pushes deeper through the mortuary complex toward the Distortion gate chamber. Combat continues; cult forces grow stronger; environmental hazards (lit lanterns, ghost-touched mossy stone, darkened crypts) intensify the gothic atmosphere.

3. **Reaching Nemo's chamber** — player reaches the gate chamber's antechamber. **Nemo is dying.** Baradus has brought him to the Necropolis for the binding ritual; Nemo's blood has been drained as part of Ting-Lu's ritual binding and the resonance conditions for Chien-Pao.

4. **Nemo's death and the Ting-Lu pass** — **Nemo passes Ting-Lu to Osrid before he dies.** His final words to Ting-Lu: *"help the prince put an end to this."* Nemo dies. (This is the project's most emotionally weighted character-death moment; see Section 7 — Nemo Korolev for full character profile.)

5. **The 7v9 final battle at the Distortion gate chamber** — **two consecutive double battles, no breaks, no healing between them:**

  **Battle 1: Player team + Ting-Lu vs Baradus's Stage 1 team (Section 8 — Baradus profile)**
  - Player's main team (6 Pokemon, possibly including Keerin if the player completed her sidequest) + Ting-Lu (recently passed to Osrid) versus Baradus's 6: Shardik (shiny Beartic, Tough Claws), Abomasnow (Snow Warning), Aurorus (Refrigerate), Articuno (Snow Cloak), Froslass (Snow Cloak), Alolan Sandslash (Slush Rush)
  - Snow weather dominant. Whatever survives Battle 1 goes into Battle 2 with no recovery.

  **Battle 2 (immediately): Player team + Ting-Lu vs Baradus's Stage 2 team (Section 8 — Baradus profile)**
  - **Wo-Chien** (Dark/Grass) — held by Sword Cult since before story start
  - **Chi-Yu** (Dark/Fire) — regenerated after Koraidon destroyed its body at Mt. Ceram
  - **Chien-Pao** (Dark/Ice) — **finally incarnated** at the Distortion gate using the cult's accumulated Dreamstone resonance + Nemo's blood-binding ritual
  - **Guzzlord** — Baradus's true ace, shiny, Dragon's Maw bred ability, signature move **Ravage** (130 BP / 85% acc / Dragon / Physical / no drawback)

6. **Conditional dialogue and KO triggers** if Crabominable is in the party (Section 9 — Crabominable Adoption Mechanic)

7. **Once all three Treasures of Ruin are defeated**, Ting-Lu (on the player's side) **chooses to initiate the sealing ritual** — its first conscious act of self-determination, an act of fidelity to Nemo's memory.

**The Sealing Cataclysm `[LOCKED — UPDATED v0.9.4]`:**

- **Silas Moray uses his psychic powers** to constrain Baradus and destroy the resonance bond Chien-Pao is forcing on him
- The combination of: destroying the resonance bond + the cosmic energy released by sealing all four Treasures simultaneously = **psychic energy beyond what humans can channel**
- **Silas dies from the psychic backlash.** His final act — what he himself would call thaumaturgy — is the only uncoerced act of love he ever performed. **Pierra is saved by Silas's death** (the cult threat that explicitly aimed at killing her is ended). The role he played in delusion for two decades is finally played for real.
- **Osrid survives the cataclysm with his memory intact.** Earlier drafts framed the sealing as causing amnesia; this is REVISED — **the sealing leaves Osrid physically alive, mentally intact, but presumed dead by Cormoria.** The cataclysm's chaos provides cover for his disappearance.
